## Epic List

### Epic 1: Inbox Foundation — Reliable Outlook Sync into a Local Mailbox

Adam can SSH into the VPS, run `mailbot sync-now`, and see emails appear in SQLite. Sync runs continuously and alerts within an hour if it stops. Establishes the repo skeleton, Docker stack, schema migrations, structured logging, and the five hard code boundaries that every later epic depends on. No LLM involved yet — this is the "scaffold + sync" milestone (build-order M1).

**FRs covered:** FR-1.1, FR-1.2, FR-1.3, FR-1.4, FR-1.5, FR-1.6, FR-1.7, FR-7.3 (established)
**NFRs:** NFR-SEC-1..6, NFR-OPS-1..6, NFR-HW-1..3, NFR-PERF-2
**AR-\*:** AR-BOOT-1..3 (incl. numpy + matplotlib pinning), AR-D7-1..2, AR-D8-1..2, AR-D9-1..2, AR-D14-1, AR-SCHEMA-2, AR-SCHEMA-6, AR-PAT-1..6, AR-DEPLOY-1, AR-DEPLOY-3

### Epic 2: Router as Cost-Discipline Center — Local LLM + Policy + Budget Guards

Every LLM call goes through one place. The cheap one is the default. There's no way for the bill to blow up. Combines F3 (Router build) and F6 (Budget guards) because every budget hook lives in `router/budget.py` and is touched on every call — separating them would mean 3+ rounds of editing the same files for no Adam-visible value at the seams. Ships local Qwen via Ollama, the 4-layer budget guard, hot-reloadable `policy.yaml`, two-queue lane scheduling, audit log, anti-loop, and `/cost`/`/pause`/`/resume`/`/budget reset` slash commands.

**FRs covered:** FR-3.1, FR-3.2, FR-3.3, FR-3.4, FR-3.5, FR-3.6, FR-3.7, FR-3.8, FR-3.9, FR-3.10, FR-6.1, FR-6.2, FR-6.3, FR-6.4, FR-6.5, FR-6.6, FR-6.7
**NFRs:** NFR-PERF-1 (chat p95 ≤ 5s for Qwen/Haiku — established here for Qwen leg)
**AR-\*:** AR-D2-1..2, AR-D10-1..3, AR-D11-1..2, AR-ERR-1..6, AR-SCHEMA-1, AR-USERMODEL-1 (acknowledgement)

### Epic 3: Ingest Pipeline — Classify Once, Cached Forever

Every new email gets classified, summarized, importance-scored, and embedded — once. The chat-time agent reads derived fields; it does not recompute. Sensitivity is the first pipeline step, and Router enforces "no other Router call until `sensitivity_at IS NOT NULL`" as a hard invariant. All ingest-task prompts (sensitivity, coarse, fine, summary, importance, action, embedding) land here. Privacy posture (VPS-as-trust-boundary) becomes provable end-to-end.

**FRs covered:** FR-2.1, FR-2.2, FR-2.3, FR-2.4, FR-2.5, FR-2.6, FR-2.7
**NFRs:** NFR-PRIV-0, NFR-PRIV-1, NFR-PRIV-2, NFR-PRIV-3, NFR-PERF-3, NFR-PERF-4
**AR-\*:** AR-ERR-6, AR-PAT-5

### Epic 4: Authorized Actions — Adam Approves Every Send and Delete

Nothing leaves the mailbox without Adam's consent. Nothing gets deleted without his consent. The 20-send/day cap is unconditional. Builds the 4-tier authorization model as a verb-API enforcement (agent cannot promote tier), the queued-intent write-back with second auth check at drain, cooling-off + `/cancel`, scoped time-bounded grants, hybrid sync conflict policy (Tier-3 ETag-strict, Tier-1/2 lenient), error-classified retry chain, and the sensitive-content API override handshake (token mint + consume + audit). All 19 action types pinned to 4 tiers in an `ActionType` enum with `tier_for()` lookup.

**FRs covered:** FR-5.1, FR-5.2, FR-5.3, FR-5.4, FR-5.5, FR-5.6, FR-5.7
**NFRs:** *(none direct — NFR-PRIV-4 closes in Epic 5)*
**AR-\*:** AR-D1-1..2, AR-D4-1..2, AR-D5-1..4, AR-D6-1..4, AR-D12-1..2, AR-SCHEMA-3, AR-SCHEMA-4, AR-SCHEMA-5

### Epic 5: Conversational Control — Discord Chat as the Defender's Voice

Adam DMs the bot "show me unread from today" and it answers within p95 ≤ 5s. He says "draft a reply to that" and Opus drafts; the send flow (built in Epic 4) handles the rest. He can `/pause` it. It talks like a defender — conservative, quiet, asking before destructive actions. Ships the Hermes integration end-to-end: Discord adapter, MCP server, `SOUL.md` persona, `AGENTS.md` operational rules, the agent-facing verb library (`find_emails`, `hydrate_email`, `get_thread`, `list_unread`, `count_emails`, `get_sender_summary`), and all chat-side prompts (`intent_parsing_chat`, `reference_resolution`, `draft_reply`, `tone_style_mirror`, `multi_turn_refinement`). User model lives in Hermes persistent memory.

**FRs covered:** FR-4.1, FR-4.2, FR-4.3 (built), FR-4.4, FR-4.5 (completed), FR-4.6, FR-4.7, FR-4.8
**NFRs:** NFR-PRIV-4, NFR-PERSONA-1, NFR-PERSONA-2, NFR-PERSONA-3
**AR-\*:** AR-USERMODEL-1, AR-DEPLOY-2

### Epic 6: Observability & Trust Signals — Adam Sees What MailBot Is Doing

Adam SSHes in, runs `mailbot status`, gets a 10-second answer. The 08:00 digest arrives. He can `/mute` categories. He can DM `/spend month` and get a PNG bar chart of cost per task posted to Discord. MailBot tracks its own response rate and goes urgent-only if it becomes noisy. Ships the four-tier notification system, anti-fatigue mechanics, the hybrid daily digest composer (`compose_digest()` verb + Hermes agent intro), the cron split (Hermes for agent jobs, mailbot-api internal scheduler for infra), VPS operator surface (`mailbot` CLI + `setup_vps.sh` + `deploy.sh` + `backup.sh` + `restore.sh`), `worker_health` heartbeats, the analytics-verbs surface (matplotlib-rendered PNGs posted to Discord), and Hermes `fallback_providers` as the emergency safety net. Closes out structured JSON logging end-to-end.

**FRs covered:** FR-7.1, FR-7.2, FR-7.3 (completed), FR-7.4, FR-7.5, FR-7.6, FR-7.7
**NFRs:** NFR-OPS-6 (Hermes fallback)
**AR-\*:** AR-D3-1, AR-D13-1, AR-DEPLOY-4, AR-DEPLOY-5, AR-SCHEMA-7, AR-ANALYTICS-1..2

### Epic 7: Production Calibration — Continuous Self-Measurement in the Wild

**Scope-cleaved 2026-06-07 (party-mode roundtable).** Original Epic 7 ("Eval & Calibration") covered both the static benchmark loop (corpus + runner + scorer + report) AND the continuous calibration loop (Sunday sampling + KL drift + shadow rollouts). Epic 9 (new) absorbed the static benchmark loop + `/model` manual override surface. Epic 7 retains the production-side continuous calibration: weekly drift detection on production traffic, Sunday production sampling for corpus growth, and shadow-mode prompt rollouts against the production stream. Epic 7 is now a hard dependent of Epic 9 — these stories all REQUIRE the corpus + runner + scorer + report from Epic 9 to already exist (the Sunday sampler samples against the corpus, KL-drift compares production distribution to corpus distribution, shadow-mode validates against scorer ground truth).

**Original Epic 7 Stories 7.1–7.4 (corpus + runner + scorer + report) MOVED to Epic 9.** Epic 7 retains Stories 7.5 (Sunday production sampling), 7.6 (weekly KL-divergence drift), and 7.7 (shadow-mode rollouts + FR-4.3 production validation).

**Epic 7 sized by net-new only.** Per Adam-decided N.5-epic policy (Epic 6.5 retro 2026-06-06, decision B3): walk-discovered defects spawn an N.5 epic rather than being absorbed; no closure-absorption reserve. The 3 prep/carry-forward stories at the top (`7-0-prep` + `7-0-f30-f31` + `7-0-c24`) are Epic 6.5 retro carry-forwards, NOT net-new Epic 7 scope; they sequence before Epic 9 begins (and therefore before Story 7.5) and ship in their own block.

**FRs covered:** FR-8.5, FR-8.6, FR-8.7, FR-4.3 (production-validated)
**NFRs:** *(none direct)*
**AR-\*:** AR-ANALYTICS-1 (numpy used by drift report)

### Epic 9: Manual Model Control & Benchmark Harness — Evidence-Backed Routing

Adam picks the model from chat. Every model assignment in `policy.yaml` is defensible with a benchmark number. Ships the `/model` slash command (one-shot per-call override + persistent per-task override via `policy.user-overrides.yaml` companion file), the frozen eval corpus (~100 hand-labeled emails + 20-item reference-resolution slice + 20 subjective anchors), the resumable cost-capped benchmark runner (via Router `force_model` — Rule I unbroken), the objective + subjective scorer (with cross-evaluator agreement calibration), the report generator (Pareto frontier + DEMOTE/PROMOTE with confidence intervals + sample-size gates), and two contract pins required for both halves (`policy.user-overrides.yaml` merge contract + `model_chosen_reason` vocabulary enum). Discharges FR-6.6's "every Opus assignment cites a benchmark" promise at first-run; closes the LLM-choice surface from "policy-by-intuition" to "policy-by-evidence." Bumps `policy.yaml` v0 → v1 at done-flip with at least one benchmark-driven routing change OR a documented Adam-signed no-change verdict.

**Epic identity:** canary against silent routing drift, so that when CP-1 ships, Adam ships a measured product, not a hoped-for one.

**FRs covered:** FR-3.3 (override surface completed via chat), FR-6.6 (evidence layer), FR-8.1, FR-8.2, FR-8.3, FR-8.4
**NFRs:** NFR-PRIV-2 (sensitivity gate inherited by `/model` — gate-coverage), NFR-PERF-1 (Haiku/Opus latency profiles measured by runner)
**AR-\*:** AR-SCHEMA-8 (`benchmark_runs`), AR-MODEL-OVERRIDE (new — `policy.user-overrides.yaml` companion-file merge), AR-COHORT-KEY (new — `(prompt_v, scorer_model, anchors_v, router_policy_v)` four-tuple), AR-ANALYTICS-1 (numpy used by scorer + Pareto frontier)

### Epic 10: Full-Perimeter Live Validation — README-as-Charter UAT

The product is what the README says it is — prove it. The README (rewritten 2026-07-04 as the main user documentation) contains executable claims: Discord command examples with expected outputs, a 12-command slash table, an operator CLI table, and a 17-row common-errors table with stable error codes. Epic 10 treats each documented example as one acceptance walk and each error row as one fault-injection case — UAT run on the LOCAL Docker stack (per Adam-decision D4: product viable locally is the top priority; VPS/CP-1 fires only on Adam's explicit green light). Ships the one pre-declared dev fix (pre_state capture + Tier-1 revert for the move-family — the only write path never live-walked and irreversible today), a qwen batch-lane quality audit, ~40-45 walk/fault cases, and closes with the README fully evidence-backed: every walked example's illustrative output replaced with real captured output under a `<!-- verified 10-x, run_id ... -->` tag, plus a complete per-row PASS/FAIL/EXCLUDED-with-reason verdict table. All other walk-discovered defects are FILED, not absorbed, per the N.5 policy.

**Epic identity:** validate the full user-facing perimeter of MailBot against README.md as the test oracle, so the docs stop being illustrative and become evidence.

**FRs covered:** none net-new — full-perimeter L3 validation of the already-shipped FR surface (F1–F8) against README claims
**NFRs:** NFR-PRIV-2 + NFR-PRIV-4 (re-verified under live perimeter walks), NFR-PERSONA-1..3 (soft-assert per doc-drift rule (b)), NFR-PERF-1 (observed under live walks)
**AR-\*:** AR-D5-\*/AR-D6-\* (queued-intent + drain seam exercised live + extended with move-family pre_state/revert), AR-ERR-1..6 (stable error-code contract validated under fault injection)

### Epic 10.5: Perimeter Defect Remediation — Fix What the Charter-UAT Found

Epic 10 walked the perimeter and found 34% of walked README promises false while every subsystem gate (1,708 tests) was green: pause is not a kill-switch (a real Graph write dispatched 259ms after `propose` while paused), the authorization contracts live in persona prose the agent freely violates, the flagship Opus draft pipeline ships unwired from chat, and documented recovery tools crash on invocation. Epic 10.5 fixes the must-fix perimeter — clusters A (safety/kill-switch), B (authorization at the API layer + sensitivity-refusal envelope), C (never-wired capabilities), D (operator recovery tooling), E (degraded-mode + estimator truth + per-answer cost footer), and the F-10-5-1 slash→plain-NL charter rewrite — as 6 dev stories, before Epic 7 calibrates a perimeter nobody can currently reach. Clusters F–G (classification quality + dead-code/UX cleanups) trail as filed backlog. Spawned at the Epic 10 retrospective (Adam R2 full must-fix, R3 before Epic 7); the fix designs for the refusal envelope (§8.5), cost footer (§8.6), and slash rewrite (§8.7) were Adam-decided at the retro.

**FRs covered:** none net-new — makes the already-shipped FR surface (F1–F8) behave as the README claims (closes the L2-green → L3-true gap for the claims Epic 10 found false)
**NFRs:** NFR-PRIV-2 + NFR-PRIV-4 (sensitivity/authorization moved from persona prose to API enforcement), NFR-SEC-\* (kill-switch coverage + no-self-mint), NFR-OPS-\* (operator recovery tooling that works)
**AR-\*:** AR-PAT-4 (refusals as errors-as-data, not exceptions on the chat path), AR-D5-\*/AR-D6-\* (authorization/drain seam hardened at the API layer)

### Epic 10.6: Capability Reachability — Make the Cheap Lane and the Draft Pipeline Reachable on the Real User Path

Epic 10.5's own live walks (and the AI-1 walk that followed the retro) surfaced the retro's central lesson one layer deeper than expected: **"wired + capable + tested" ≠ "reached on the real user path."** AI-1 proved Qwen CAN tool-call and shipped the adapter + router capability gate (done + committed) — yet every live Discord chat tool-call still routes to haiku, not Qwen (DB `router_calls` truth), so the cheap local lane the whole cost thesis depends on never actually acts. In parallel, F-10-5-11 shows the flagship Opus draft pipeline is built + MCP-registered but the persona hand-writes drafts in haiku (0 Opus `draft_reply` rows across three walks). Epic 10.6 closes the **reachability last mile** — routing/policy + persona-dispatch — so the capabilities Epic 10.5 fixed and AI-1 restored are actually reached by a real user turn, plus the Graph-auth-at-drain infra fix that currently makes every action-taking walk silently fail, and the aging `scratch/` ruff chore. Spawned at the Epic 10.5 retrospective (2026-07-11); sequenced BEFORE Epic 7 for the same reason as 10.5 — Epic 7 calibrates routing quality, which is meaningless while the cheap lane can't act and the flagship draft path is unreachable.

**FRs covered:** none net-new — makes the already-shipped FR surface (F1–F8, esp. the Router cost-routing + draft pipeline) actually reachable from the live chat path (closes the L2-green/L3-capable → L3-reached gap AI-1's walk found)
**NFRs:** NFR-COST-\* (the cheap local lane must carry real work for the cost thesis to hold — memory `project_local_model_is_safety_net.md`), NFR-OPS-\* (Graph-auth drain must work or no action completes)
**AR-\*:** AR-PAT-4 (errors-as-data on the chat path preserved), Router↔policy cost-routing seam, persona/dispatch-contract (hermes-config) reach

### Epic 10.7: Qwen Tool-Call Fidelity — Make the Local Lane Actually Invoke the Right Tool

Epic 10.6 climbed the reachability ladder to its top rung and found one more: **routed → usable → *faithful*.** 10.6.4 made a full qwen tool-call turn *complete* within budget; 10.6.5 made the 26 MailBot verbs *reach* qwen's function list (ListTools-proven). But the same live walk exposed that qwen — on a clean surface, at temperature 0 — still (a) mis-**selects** (picks `send_message` over `find_emails`) and (b) emits the tool call as literal `<tool_call>{…}</tool_call>` **text** in the response content instead of a structured function-call object, so the harness sees `tool_calls_count=0` and dispatches nothing. That is the last thing standing between "the cheap lane got the turn" and "the cheap lane did the work" — the founding cost thesis rendered as its final gate (Epic 10.6 done-flip clause 3b, reassigned here per Adam D1 at the 2026-07-15 retro). This is a **model/harness fidelity** problem, not a routing, latency, or surface problem — the sibling class to 10.6.1 (argument fidelity) and 10.6.4 (latency). The epic opens with a **characterization spike** (Charlie's retro flag: is this a prompt-template/harness-parser fix, or Qwen-3B hitting its capability ceiling?) whose finding gates whether the rest is "tune the harness" or "swap the local model." Spawned at the Epic 10.6 retrospective (2026-07-14 walk + Adam D2 2026-07-15); sequenced BEFORE Epic 7 — calibrating routing quality (Epic 7) while the cheap lane can't reliably invoke a tool would calibrate a product nobody can use.

**FRs covered:** none net-new — makes the already-reached cheap-lane tool-call path (Epic 10.6) actually *fire* a tool from a real user turn (closes the L3-reached → L3-*faithful* gap the 10.6.5 walk found)
**NFRs:** NFR-COST-\* (the cheap local lane must carry real tool-driven work or the cost thesis fails — memory `project_local_model_is_safety_net.md`), NFR-PERF-1 (chat p95 preserved — temp-0 argument fidelity is load-bearing, `models.py:596-628`)
**AR-\*:** AR-PAT-4 (errors-as-data preserved), OllamaAdapter tool-call parse/translate seam (`models.py`), Router capability-gate + dispatch seam (`router.py`), Hermes tool-surface/persona reach (`hermes-config/`)

---

## Epic 1 Detail — Inbox Foundation

This epic establishes the scaffold every other epic depends on: repo + dependency pinning, Docker stack, SQLite layer with WAL + migrations, hard code boundaries enforced from day one, Microsoft Graph OAuth bootstrap + token rotation persistence, delta sync with idempotent upserts, the two-process container topology, and the continuous worker with sync-health alarm. After this epic, Adam can SSH into the VPS, run `mailbot sync-now`, and see emails appear in SQLite. No LLM is involved yet — sync is pure Python + Graph + SQL.

### Story 1.1: Repo scaffold + dependency pinning

As Adam,
I want a clean Python package skeleton with all runtime dependencies pinned in `requirements.txt`,
So that every later story builds on a known, reproducible foundation and `pip install -r requirements.txt && pytest -q` succeeds on a fresh machine.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** an empty repo directory
**When** the bootstrap sequence runs (`git init`; `python -m venv .venv`; activate venv; create `requirements.txt`; create the `mailbot_api/` + `evals/` + `benchmark/` + `scripts/` + `hermes-config/` + `docker/` + `tests/` + `docs/` + `router/` directory tree per architecture.md §2; create `pyproject.toml`, `Makefile`, `.env.example`, `.gitignore`, `.dockerignore`, `.editorconfig`, `README.md`)
**Then** the directory tree matches the architecture document's pinned layout exactly
**And** `requirements.txt` contains pinned entries: `fastapi==0.136.1`, `uvicorn[standard]`, `pydantic>=2`, `anthropic==0.105.2`, `ollama==0.6.2`, `mcp==1.27.2`, `numpy`, `matplotlib`, `httpx`, `pyyaml`, `watchfiles`, `pytest`, `pytest-asyncio`, `ruff`, `mypy`
**And** `pip install -r requirements.txt` completes without errors on Python 3.12
**And** `pytest -q` runs to completion with zero collected tests (no errors)
**And** `.gitignore` blocks `.env`, `*.key`, `*.pem`, `__pycache__`, `.venv`, `*.db`
**And** `.env.example` lists all required env keys (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OUTLOOK_CLIENT_ID`, `OUTLOOK_CLIENT_SECRET`, `OUTLOOK_TENANT_ID`, `OUTLOOK_REFRESH_TOKEN`, `MAILBOT_DB_PATH`, `OLLAMA_URL`, `MAILBOT_ROUTER_KEY`) with one-line comments and no values
**And** `pyproject.toml` configures `ruff`, `mypy --strict`, and `pytest` per architecture §4

**Given** the scaffolded repo
**When** `ruff check .` and `mypy --strict mailbot_api/` are run
**Then** both exit with status 0 (no rules triggered on the empty scaffold)

### Story 1.2: Docker stack scaffolding with health endpoints

As Adam,
I want `docker compose up` to bring up the three-container stack (`mailbot-hermes`, `mailbot-api`, `ollama`) with named volumes and a working `/health` endpoint on `mailbot-api`,
So that I can verify the deployment topology end-to-end before any business logic is written.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the Story 1.1 scaffold
**When** `docker/Dockerfile.mailbot-api`, `docker/entrypoint.sh`, `docker-compose.yml`, and `docker-compose.override.yml` are added per architecture.md §2 and §3
**Then** `docker-compose.yml` declares three services: `mailbot-hermes` (`nousresearch/hermes-agent:latest`), `mailbot-api` (built from `docker/Dockerfile.mailbot-api`), and `ollama` (`ollama/ollama:latest`)
**And** all three services connect to a `mailbot-net` Docker network
**And** four named volumes are declared: `mailbot_db`, `mailbot_ollama`, `mailbot_hermes_data`, `mailbot_logs`
**And** `mailbot-api` exposes a `GET /health` endpoint that returns HTTP 200 with `{"ok": true}` and a `GET /v1/health` endpoint with the same shape
**And** `docker-compose.override.yml` bind-mounts `mailbot_api/`, `router/`, `prompts/`, and `verbs/` into `mailbot-api` for dev hot-reload, and exposes ports `8000:8000` and `11434:11434`

**Given** the Docker stack is running (`make local` invokes `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.override.yml up`)
**When** `curl http://localhost:8000/health` is executed from the host
**Then** the response is HTTP 200 with body `{"ok": true}` within 5 seconds of stack startup
**And** `curl http://localhost:11434/api/tags` returns HTTP 200 (Ollama is reachable)
**And** `docker compose ps` shows all three containers as `running` and `healthy`

**Given** the running stack
**When** `docker compose down && docker compose up` is run
**Then** all named volumes survive the cycle (verifiable by `docker volume ls` showing the same volume IDs before and after)
**And** no data on the volumes is lost (verifiable by writing a marker file to `mailbot_logs` before `down`, observing it present after `up`)

### Story 1.3: SQLite layer with WAL, executor writes, and migration runner

As Adam,
I want the SQLite connection layer with WAL mode, `busy_timeout=5000`, async wrappers, and a migrations runner that applies numbered `.sql` files at startup,
So that every later story has a working `db.fetchone()` / `db.execute_write()` interface and a clean way to add tables.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `mailbot_api/db/connection.py` and `mailbot_api/db/queries.py` are implemented
**When** the FastAPI app starts up
**Then** the SQLite connection is opened against `MAILBOT_DB_PATH` (from env)
**And** every connection applies pragmas: `journal_mode=WAL`, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `busy_timeout=5000`, `foreign_keys=ON`
**And** `connection.py` exposes async `fetchone(query, params)`, `fetchall(query, params)`, and `execute_write(query, params)` functions
**And** `execute_write` dispatches through `asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor(None, …)` so writes never block the event loop
**And** reads run synchronously on the event loop (sub-millisecond under WAL)
**And** every connection is acquired per-call (no shared connections across awaits) and closed promptly

**Given** the directory `mailbot_api/db/migrations/` exists
**When** a migration runner is implemented and run at startup
**Then** the runner reads `_migrations` table (creates it if missing) and applies any `NNN_*.sql` file not yet recorded, in numeric order
**And** each successful application inserts a row `(filename, applied_at)` into `_migrations`
**And** a failed migration aborts startup with a structured log line and a non-zero exit code (no partial application)

**Given** an initial migration `mailbot_api/db/migrations/001_init.sql` is added
**When** the migration runs on a fresh DB
**Then** it creates the `emails`, `threads`, `senders`, and `sync_state` tables with the columns defined in architecture.md §2 (including derived-field companion columns `*_prompt_v`, `*_conf`, `*_model`, `*_at` per FR-2.1, and `emails.change_marker` per AR-SCHEMA-2)
**And** all timestamps are stored as `TEXT` in UTC ISO-8601 with `Z` suffix (per AR-PAT-3)
**And** table names are plural `snake_case` and column names are `snake_case` (per AR-PAT-2)
**And** indexes follow the `ix_<table>_<col1>_<col2>` convention

**Given** the migration has applied
**When** `db.fetchone("SELECT 1")` is invoked from an async context
**Then** it returns `(1,)` and does not raise

### Story 1.4: Hard code boundaries + structured JSON logging

As Adam,
I want the five hard code boundaries (Router / sync / db / config / audit) enforced by ruff lint rules from day one, plus structured JSON logging with sanitization,
So that any later story attempting to bypass a boundary fails CI rather than being caught in review.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `mailbot_api/config.py` is implemented
**When** any code in `mailbot_api/` needs to read an environment variable or a secret
**Then** it calls `get_secret(name: str) -> str` from `mailbot_api/config.py`
**And** `get_secret` reads from `.env` via `os.environ[name]` and raises `SecretMissing(name)` if absent
**And** `os.environ[...]` does not appear anywhere else in `mailbot_api/` source code

**Given** ruff is configured in `pyproject.toml`
**When** `ruff check .` is run
**Then** custom rules (or import-checks via `flake8-tidy-imports` / `ruff` allow/deny lists) fail the lint pass for:

- `os.environ` imports/reads outside `mailbot_api/config.py`
- `import ollama` or `from ollama` outside `mailbot_api/router/models.py`
- `import anthropic` or `from anthropic` outside `mailbot_api/router/models.py`
- `requests` calls targeting `graph.microsoft.com` outside `mailbot_api/sync/graph_client.py`
- `sqlite3.connect()` outside `mailbot_api/db/connection.py`
- raw SQL literals (string containing `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` keywords with table identifiers) outside `mailbot_api/db/queries.py`
- `print()` calls outside `scripts/`
- `datetime.utcnow()` anywhere (mandate `datetime.now(timezone.utc)`)

**And** a deliberately-violating fixture file in `tests/fixtures/lint_violations/` triggers each rule when added to the lint root

**Given** `mailbot_api/observability/logging.py` is implemented
**When** any module emits a log via the project logger
**Then** the output to stdout is a single JSON object per line with fields: `ts` (UTC ISO-8601 with `Z`), `level`, `module`, `event` (dotted namespace, e.g. `db.migration.applied`), and any additional context
**And** a sanitizer pass strips: any string matching `Bearer\s+[A-Za-z0-9._-]+`, any URL containing `?` followed by a `token`/`code`/`access_token` query param value, any string matching `sk-[A-Za-z0-9-]{20,}`, any file path containing `.env` or `.key` or `.pem`
**And** the logger writes to stdout only (Docker captures it); no file handlers configured

**Given** the logging module is loaded
**When** a log line is emitted with a known-secret value injected as context
**Then** the secret value is redacted in the emitted JSON (regression-tested in `tests/unit/observability/test_logging_sanitizer.py`)

### Story 1.5: Microsoft Graph client + OAuth bootstrap from `.env`

As Adam,
I want a Microsoft Graph HTTP client that authenticates using the refresh token in `.env` and fetches the authenticated user's mailbox metadata,
So that the auth pipeline is proven end-to-end before any sync logic is written.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `OUTLOOK_CLIENT_ID`, `OUTLOOK_CLIENT_SECRET`, `OUTLOOK_TENANT_ID`, and `OUTLOOK_REFRESH_TOKEN` are present in `.env`
**When** `mailbot_api/sync/graph_client.py` is implemented and a one-shot test script (`scripts/check_graph_auth.py`) is run
**Then** the client exchanges the refresh token for an access token via the Microsoft identity platform (`POST https://login.microsoftonline.com/{tenant}/oauth2/v2.0/token`)
**And** the access token is held in memory only (never written to disk in this story — persistence lands in Story 1.6)
**And** the client issues `GET https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/me` with the access token in the `Authorization: Bearer …` header
**And** the script prints the authenticated user's `displayName` and `userPrincipalName` to stdout (via the structured logger, sanitized)
**And** if the refresh token is invalid or expired, the script exits non-zero with a structured log line `event="oauth.refresh.failed"` and a human-readable message in `docs/auth-recovery.md`'s anchor format

**Given** the Graph client is implemented
**When** any code outside `mailbot_api/sync/` attempts to `import requests` targeting `graph.microsoft.com` or call the Graph base URL
**Then** ruff (per Story 1.4) blocks the import or call

**Given** the client makes an authenticated call
**When** the request is logged
**Then** the URL is sanitized to remove any query-string tokens (per Story 1.4 sanitizer)
**And** the Authorization header is never logged in any form

### Story 1.6: `oauth_state` table + token rotation persistence

As Adam,
I want refreshed Graph access tokens and rotated refresh tokens to persist to an `oauth_state` SQLite row so they survive container restarts,
So that I don't have to hand-copy a new refresh token into `.env` every time Microsoft rotates the credential.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 1.3's migration runner is in place
**When** migration `002_oauth_state.sql` is added and runs at startup
**Then** the `oauth_state` table is created with columns: `provider` (TEXT PRIMARY KEY), `refresh_token` (TEXT), `access_token` (TEXT, nullable), `access_expires_at` (TEXT, UTC ISO-8601, nullable), `last_rotated_at` (TEXT, UTC ISO-8601, nullable), `rotation_count` (INTEGER, default 0)

**Given** `mailbot_api/sync/oauth.py` is implemented
**When** the sync layer needs a Graph access token
**Then** it first reads the `oauth_state` row for `provider="microsoft_graph"` via `db.fetchone(...)`
**And** if the row exists and `access_expires_at` is in the future, returns the cached access token
**And** if the row exists and the access token is expired, exchanges the stored `refresh_token` for a new access token (and possibly a new refresh token), then writes the new values back to `oauth_state` via `db.execute_write(...)`, increments `rotation_count`, and updates `last_rotated_at`
**And** if the row does not exist (first run after a fresh deploy), reads `OUTLOOK_REFRESH_TOKEN` from `.env` via `get_secret(...)`, performs the initial token exchange, and writes the result to `oauth_state` (the `.env` value is the bootstrap seed only)

**Given** a token rotation succeeds
**When** the rotation event is logged
**Then** the structured log line has `event="oauth.token.rotated"`, `level="info"`, `rotation_count=<new value>`, and no token material in the payload

**Given** a token rotation fails with `invalid_grant`
**When** the failure is detected
**Then** a structured log line with `event="oauth.refresh.failed"` and `level="error"` is emitted
**And** the failure is recorded so Story 1.8's sync-health alarm can surface it within 1 hour
**And** `docs/auth-recovery.md` contains the human-readable recovery procedure (re-run the dev-box auth flow, hand-copy the new refresh token to `.env`, restart the container — the bootstrap seed flow re-engages)

**Given** the rotation flow is implemented
**When** `scripts/backup.sh` runs the nightly backup
**Then** `oauth_state` is included in the backup tarball (i.e. the SQLite `.backup` covers all tables)
**And** `.env` is excluded (per NFR-SEC-6)

### Story 1.7: Delta sync worker + idempotent upserts

As Adam,
I want `mailbot sync-now` to fetch the Graph delta since the last successful sync and upsert new/changed/deleted email rows into SQLite idempotently,
So that running sync twice with no inbox changes produces zero writes.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Stories 1.5 and 1.6 are complete (Graph client + `oauth_state`)
**When** `mailbot_api/sync/sync_worker.py` is implemented and `scripts/mailbot sync-now` is invoked
**Then** the worker reads the current `delta_link` from `sync_state` (or null on first sync)
**And** issues `GET https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/me/mailFolders/inbox/messages/delta` (with the stored link on subsequent runs)
**And** iterates through all delta pages until reaching the next-page sentinel
**And** for each returned message: upserts a row into `emails` keyed on `graph_id`, capturing `change_marker` (Graph's `@odata.etag` or equivalent), `received_at` (UTC ISO-8601 with `Z`), `from_address`, `from_display_name`, `subject`, `body_preview`, `has_attachments` (boolean only — bodies and attachments are NOT fetched per FR-1.7)
**And** upserts the corresponding `sender` and `thread` rows (FR-2.7's `sender_reputation_summary` derivation is deferred to Epic 3 — only the row presence is established here)
**And** for any message marked deleted in the delta (`@removed` annotation), sets `emails.deleted_at = <now UTC>` rather than deleting the row (FR-1.3 soft-delete)
**And** writes the new `delta_link` to `sync_state` only after the full batch completes successfully

**Given** the sync worker has just run successfully
**When** `mailbot sync-now` is invoked again with no inbox changes
**Then** the delta query returns zero messages
**And** zero rows are written to `emails`, `threads`, or `senders` (verifiable by SQL row-count before/after)
**And** the `sync_state.delta_link` is updated (the link itself rotates even when results are empty)
**And** the worker emits one structured log line with `event="sync.completed"`, `messages_seen=0`, `messages_upserted=0`, `duration_ms=<...>`

**Given** the same Graph delta is delivered twice (simulated via fixture or replay)
**When** the worker processes it twice
**Then** the second pass writes zero new rows (idempotent on `(graph_id, change_marker)` per FR-1.4)

**Given** the first-ever sync against a large inbox
**When** the worker runs
**Then** it processes all delta pages without timing out
**And** writes a progress log line every 500 messages
**And** a single sync run handles 10,000+ messages without OOM on the 8 GB VPS (verifiable by RAM measurement)

**Given** a Graph 429 (rate limit) is returned mid-sync
**When** the worker hits it
**Then** it respects the `Retry-After` header and resumes
**And** logs `event="sync.throttled"` with `wait_seconds=<...>`
**And** does NOT advance `delta_link` until the batch completes

### Story 1.8: Two-process container entrypoint + continuous sync-health alarm

As Adam,
I want the `mailbot-api` container to run two processes (uvicorn + worker) from a single entrypoint, with the worker running sync continuously every 4 minutes and surfacing a sync-health alarm if no successful sync completes within 1 hour,
So that the inbox stays fresh unattended and I find out within an hour when it doesn't.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 1.7's sync worker is complete
**When** `mailbot_api/worker.py` and `docker/entrypoint.sh` are implemented
**Then** `entrypoint.sh` backgrounds `python -m mailbot_api.worker` and foregrounds `uvicorn mailbot_api.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000`
**And** the worker process runs an asyncio loop that schedules `sync_worker.run_once()` every 4 minutes (aligned to Anthropic cache TTL per FR-1.1)
**And** the loop catches and structured-logs any exception from `run_once()` without exiting the worker process (continuous-operation guarantee)
**And** the worker writes a row to `worker_health` (table created by a migration `004_worker_health.sql` in this story) on every successful sync iteration with `(component="sync", last_heartbeat_at, last_outcome="ok", last_error=NULL)`

**Given** `worker_health` exists with columns `(component TEXT PRIMARY KEY, last_heartbeat_at TEXT, last_outcome TEXT, last_error TEXT NULL)`
**When** a sync iteration fails
**Then** the worker writes `(component="sync", last_heartbeat_at=<now>, last_outcome="failed", last_error=<sanitized message>)` instead of upserting an "ok" row
**And** the failure is logged with `event="sync.failed"` and `level="error"`

**Given** the worker has been running
**When** the FastAPI `GET /health` endpoint is called
**Then** the response includes `sync_last_heartbeat_at`, `sync_last_outcome`, and a computed `sync_minutes_since_last_ok` (read from `worker_health` via `db.fetchone(...)`)
**And** if `sync_minutes_since_last_ok > 60`, the endpoint still returns HTTP 200 but the response body sets `sync_health_alarm: true`
**And** a structured log line `event="sync.health.alarm"` with `level="error"` is emitted on the first iteration where the alarm tripped (only once per alarm episode — debounced)

**Given** the sync-health alarm has tripped
**When** the chat-notification path is invoked
**Then** in this story, a placeholder hook `notifications.send_urgent("sync stale > 1h")` writes the alert to a `notifications_pending` log file in the `mailbot_logs` volume (Epic 5 replaces this with the real Discord-via-Hermes path; the interface signature stays stable)
**And** the alarm hook fires at most once per stale episode (resets when a successful sync resumes)

**Given** the two-process container is running
**When** `docker exec mailbot-api ps -ef` is inspected
**Then** both `uvicorn` and `python -m mailbot_api.worker` processes appear
**And** killing the worker process (`docker exec mailbot-api kill <worker_pid>`) causes the container's entrypoint to exit (signaling a restart to Docker) — i.e., the worker is not silently swallowed

**Given** `make local` is running the dev stack
**When** Story 1.7's sync runs successfully end-to-end against a real Outlook test account
**Then** new emails appear in the local SQLite within ≤ 4 minutes of arriving in Outlook
**And** `scripts/mailbot status` (placeholder shell that just `SELECT`s from `worker_health` for now — Epic 6 builds the full CLI) returns within 10 seconds with `component=sync, last_outcome=ok, minutes_since=<small number>`

---

## Epic 2 Detail — Router as Cost-Discipline Center

This epic operationalizes Rule Ω: every LLM call in the system flows through one place that enforces budget, lane priority, response caching, audit logging, and sensitivity preconditions. By the end, Adam can `curl /v1/chat/completions`, watch a Qwen call complete, see a `router_calls` row written, DM `/cost` and get the breakdown, and trip the monthly cap to verify degraded mode demotes Opus → Haiku → Qwen automatically. F3 (Router build) and F6 (Budget guards) are bundled because every budget hook lives in `router/budget.py` and is touched on every call — separating them would mean editing the same files three or more times.

### Story 2.1: `router_calls` audit table + `RouterResult` / `RouterError` data shapes

As Adam,
I want the `router_calls` audit table, the `RouterResult` and `RouterError` Pydantic shapes, the stable `ErrorCode` enum, and the single `record_router_call()` writer function in place before any Router code is written,
So that every later story records calls into a known schema with no ad-hoc shape variation.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 1.3's migration runner is in place
**When** migration `005_router_calls.sql` is added and runs at startup
**Then** the `router_calls` table is created with columns: `id` (INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT), `ts` (TEXT, UTC ISO-8601 with `Z`), `task_type` (TEXT), `prompt_version` (TEXT), `model_chosen` (TEXT), `model_chosen_reason` (TEXT — one of `policy`/`override`/`degraded`/`escalated_from_<X>`), `tokens_in` (INTEGER), `tokens_out` (INTEGER), `cached_tokens_in` (INTEGER, default 0), `cost_usd_estimated` (REAL), `latency_ms` (INTEGER), `outcome` (TEXT — one of `ok`/`retry_recovered`/`escalated`/`failed`), `caller_verb` (TEXT, nullable), `caller_origin` (TEXT — populated per AR-D2-2; placeholder default `unknown` until Story 2.10), `email_id` (TEXT, nullable), `sensitivity_grant_id` (TEXT, nullable — Epic 4 populates this), `sensitivity_grant_minted_at` (TEXT, nullable)
**And** indexes `ix_router_calls_ts`, `ix_router_calls_task_type_model_chosen`, and `ix_router_calls_email_id` are created

**Given** `mailbot_api/router/errors.py` is implemented
**When** the `ErrorCode` enum is defined
**Then** it contains members: `SCHEMA_VALIDATION_FAILED`, `TIMEOUT`, `BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, `PER_CALL_THRESHOLD_EXCEEDED`, `PROVIDER_ERROR`, `MONTHLY_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, `DEGRADED_MODE_BLOCKED`, `LOOP_DETECTED`, `SENSITIVITY_BLOCKS_API`, `NEEDS_SENSITIVITY_CONFIRMATION`, `SENSITIVITY_NOT_CLASSIFIED`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `STATE_DRIFT_ETAG`, `TARGET_DELETED`, `STATE_DRIFT_NOOP`
**And** `RouterError` is a Pydantic v2 model with fields: `code: ErrorCode`, `message: str`, `model_attempted: list[str]`, `retryable: bool`
**And** `RouterResult` is a Pydantic v2 model with fields: `ok: bool`, `output: BaseModel | None`, `error: RouterError | None`, `cost_usd: float`, `latency_ms: int`, `tokens_in: int`, `tokens_out: int`, `cached_tokens_in: int`, `model_used: str`
**And** `sanitize_error(exc: Exception) -> str` strips URLs containing tokens, file paths matching `.env`/`.key`/`.pem`, and stack frames, returning a single-line redacted message

**Given** `mailbot_api/observability/audit.py` is implemented
**When** `record_router_call(call: RouterCallRow)` is invoked
**Then** the row is written via `db.execute_write(...)` (executor path per AR-D8-1)
**And** the write happens inside the Router's `finally` block in later stories — but the function itself is testable in isolation by passing a constructed `RouterCallRow`
**And** ruff (per Story 1.4) blocks any `INSERT INTO router_calls` SQL outside `mailbot_api/observability/audit.py`

**Given** the boundary is established
**When** `tests/unit/observability/test_audit.py` exercises `record_router_call` with a synthetic row
**Then** the row appears in `router_calls` with all fields preserved
**And** UTC timestamps round-trip without timezone loss
**And** the `model_chosen_reason` field rejects values outside the stable set (Pydantic validation)

### Story 2.2: `policy.yaml` schema + loader + watchfiles hot-reload

As Adam,
I want `policy.yaml` to be the single source of truth for per-task routing decisions, with a Pydantic `PolicyTable` schema, a loader that validates on every read, and `watchfiles`-driven hot-reload that swaps the in-memory snapshot atomically (or leaves the running policy in place if validation fails),
So that I can tune routing decisions by editing one file with no restart and no risk of a malformed YAML breaking the running system.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `router/policy.yaml` exists with at least the `coarse_class`, `sensitivity_class`, and `draft_reply` entries from the brainstorm's policy-v0 starter
**When** `mailbot_api/router/policy.py` is implemented
**Then** a `PolicyEntry` Pydantic model defines per-task fields: `model: str`, `prompt_version: str`, `escalate: bool`, `max_tokens_out: int`, `lane: Literal["interactive", "batch"]`, `sensitivity: Literal["normal", "sensitive", "confidential", "any"]`, `notes: str | None`, `demotion_hypothesis: str | None`, `promotion_hypothesis: str | None`
**And** `PolicyTable` is a Pydantic model with `tasks: dict[str, PolicyEntry]` and `version: str`
**And** `load_policy(path: Path) -> PolicyTable` parses YAML via `pyyaml`, validates against `PolicyTable`, and raises `PolicyValidationError(details)` on failure

**Given** the FastAPI app has started up and `load_policy(...)` succeeded
**When** any code calls `get_policy()`
**Then** it returns the currently-loaded `PolicyTable` instance from a module-level reference

**Given** `policy.yaml` is edited on disk while the app runs
**When** `watchfiles` detects the change
**Then** the watcher re-reads the file, validates it, and atomically swaps the in-memory snapshot on success (single reference assignment)
**And** emits a structured log line `event="policy.reloaded"` with the new `version` field value
**And** on validation failure, emits `event="policy.reload.failed"` with the sanitized error message and **does not** swap the in-memory snapshot (the previously-running policy stays in effect)

**Given** a Router call is in flight when a reload happens
**When** the call resumes after the swap
**Then** it continues to use the policy snapshot captured at dispatch time (no mid-call re-lookup) — verifiable by injecting a 200 ms sleep into a fake adapter and asserting the post-call `prompt_version` matches the pre-swap policy
**And** subsequent dispatches use the new policy

**Given** the watcher is running
**When** the FastAPI app shuts down
**Then** the watcher stops cleanly (no zombie thread or task)

**Given** `policy.yaml` parsing fails at initial startup
**When** the app boots
**Then** startup aborts with a structured log line and a non-zero exit code (we never serve traffic against an unvalidated policy)

### Story 2.3: Ollama adapter + Qwen local-model serving

As Adam,
I want a thin `OllamaAdapter` in `router/models.py` that calls Qwen 2.5 3B (Q4_K_M) via the `ollama` Python client and returns a normalized response shape,
So that the local-LLM leg works in isolation before being plumbed into the Router's failure chain.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the `ollama` container is running per Story 1.2 and `OLLAMA_URL` is set in `.env`
**When** the `ollama` container starts
**Then** `qwen2.5:3b-instruct-q4_K_M` and `nomic-embed-text` are pre-pulled (via a small post-start script or a model-warming step in `docker-compose.yml` / a one-shot job) and `ollama list` shows both models present

**Given** `mailbot_api/router/models.py` is implemented
**When** `OllamaAdapter(model_id="qwen2.5:3b-instruct-q4_K_M", base_url=<from config>)` is constructed
**Then** ruff (per Story 1.4) permits `import ollama` only inside `mailbot_api/router/models.py`
**And** the adapter exposes `async def call(system: str, user: str, max_tokens_out: int, temperature: float = 0.0) -> AdapterResponse` returning a normalized Pydantic shape with fields: `text: str`, `tokens_in: int`, `tokens_out: int`, `cached_tokens_in: int` (always 0 for Ollama), `latency_ms: int`, `raw: dict` (provider raw payload for debugging)
**And** the adapter applies a 30-second hard timeout (FR-3.4 local-call timeout) and raises `AdapterTimeout` on breach
**And** any non-timeout adapter exception is wrapped as `AdapterProviderError(sanitized_message)` — never re-raised raw

**Given** the Qwen model is loaded
**When** `await adapter.call(system="You are concise.", user="Reply with the word OK and nothing else.", max_tokens_out=8)` is invoked
**Then** the response `text` contains "OK" (allowing trailing whitespace)
**And** `tokens_in` and `tokens_out` are populated from the Ollama response
**And** `latency_ms` is recorded
**And** the call returns within p95 ≤ 5s on the 2-vCPU VPS hardware (per NFR-PERF-3 — measured across 20 sequential calls)

**Given** the adapter is in place
**When** `tests/unit/router/test_ollama_adapter.py` runs against a mocked Ollama HTTP server (httpx mock)
**Then** timeout behavior, error wrapping, and response normalization are all covered
**And** no `tests/integration/` test for this adapter requires a real Ollama container (kept as an opt-in fixture)

### Story 2.4: `ask_router()` core orchestration — dispatch, timeout, schema validation, retry, escalate

As Adam,
I want `ask_router(task_type, content, force_model=None, max_cost_usd=None) -> RouterResult` to be the single agent-facing LLM entry point, with the layered failure chain (timeout → schema validation → single retry with stricter prompt → escalate-to-next-tier or return structured error),
So that every LLM call in the system goes through one cost-disciplined path and the agent never sees raw exceptions.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Stories 2.1, 2.2, and 2.3 are complete
**When** `mailbot_api/router/router.py` and `mailbot_api/verbs/ask_router.py` are implemented
**Then** `ask_router(task_type, content, force_model=None, max_cost_usd=None) -> RouterResult` is the only public function in `mailbot_api/router/__init__.py`'s exported API for LLM dispatch
**And** the function captures a policy snapshot at dispatch time via `get_policy()` (per AR-D11-2 mid-call race semantics)
**And** the function resolves the prompt module via `prompts/<task_type>/<prompt_version>.py` and loads `SYSTEM`, `USER_TEMPLATE`, `OUTPUT_SCHEMA`
**And** the model selection is: `force_model` if provided (logged with `model_chosen_reason="override"`), else `policy.tasks[task_type].model` (logged with `model_chosen_reason="policy"`)
**And** the call dispatches to the appropriate adapter via a `ModelAdapter` registry (Ollama adapter from Story 2.3; Anthropic adapter slot reserved for Story 2.6)

**Given** an `ask_router` call dispatches successfully and the adapter response parses cleanly against `OUTPUT_SCHEMA`
**When** the function returns
**Then** the result is `RouterResult(ok=True, output=<parsed model>, error=None, cost_usd=<computed>, latency_ms=<...>, tokens_in/out/cached_in=<...>, model_used=<...>)`
**And** `record_router_call(...)` writes a row to `router_calls` with `outcome="ok"` inside the function's `finally` block — never lost

**Given** the adapter raises `AdapterTimeout`
**When** the failure chain runs
**Then** the function returns `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=TIMEOUT, ...))` immediately (no retry on timeout)
**And** `record_router_call` writes a row with `outcome="failed"`

**Given** the adapter returns successfully but the response fails `OUTPUT_SCHEMA.model_validate(...)`
**When** the failure chain runs
**Then** the Router performs **one retry** with a stricter prompt (the `USER_TEMPLATE` is wrapped with "Your previous reply was not valid JSON matching the schema. Reply only with valid JSON matching this schema: SCHEMA_DUMP")
**And** if the retry succeeds → `RouterResult(ok=True, ...)` with `outcome="retry_recovered"`
**And** if the retry also fails → consult `policy.tasks[task_type].escalate`:

- `escalate=True` → call the next-tier model (Qwen → Haiku, Haiku → Opus); on success → `outcome="escalated"`, `model_chosen_reason="escalated_from_<X>"`; on failure → `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=SCHEMA_VALIDATION_FAILED, ...))`
- `escalate=False` → `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=SCHEMA_VALIDATION_FAILED, ...))` immediately

**Given** the adapter raises `AdapterProviderError`
**When** the failure chain runs
**Then** the function returns `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=PROVIDER_ERROR, ...))` with the sanitized message
**And** `record_router_call` writes a row with `outcome="failed"`

**Given** any exception other than the documented adapter exceptions occurs inside `ask_router`
**When** the boundary catches it
**Then** the exception is converted to `RouterError(code=PROVIDER_ERROR, message=sanitize_error(exc))` and returned as `RouterResult(ok=False, ...)` — no exception ever crosses the verb boundary to the agent (AR-PAT-4 error-as-data)

**Given** the verb interface is in place
**When** `tests/unit/router/test_router.py` runs against a fake `ModelAdapter` that yields scripted responses (success / timeout / bad-schema / provider error)
**Then** all branches of the failure chain are covered with assertions on `outcome`, `error.code`, and the `router_calls` row written

### Story 2.5: Two-queue lane scheduling + rate limits + concurrency semaphore

As Adam,
I want the Router to maintain two asyncio queues (`interactive_q` with strict priority over `batch_q`), enforce per-lane rate limits at enqueue time (chat 60/hr, ingest 300/hr, escalations 20/hr → `rate_limited` error), and a per-provider concurrency semaphore (default 4 concurrent Anthropic calls),
So that chat-driven calls never wait behind ingest backlogs and a surprise burst can't trigger 429s from the provider.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 2.4's Router is in place
**When** `mailbot_api/router/lanes.py` is implemented
**Then** two `asyncio.Queue` instances are created: `interactive_q` (high priority) and `batch_q` (low priority)
**And** `ask_router` accepts an internal `lane` parameter (resolved from `policy.tasks[task_type].lane` if not overridden) and enqueues the call accordingly
**And** a single worker pool (default size = 8) drains `interactive_q` first; only pulls from `batch_q` when `interactive_q` is empty
**And** in-flight dispatches do not yield priority — preemption is at enqueue/dequeue only

**Given** `mailbot_api/router/limits.py` is implemented
**When** an `ask_router` call attempts to enqueue
**Then** a sliding-window rate limiter checks per-lane / per-caller counts: chat lane → 60 enqueues/hr; ingest lane → 300 enqueues/hr; escalations (any dispatch with `model_chosen_reason="escalated_from_<X>"`) → 20/hr
**And** on breach, the call returns `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=RATE_LIMITED, retryable=True, ...))` **immediately** (does not enter the queue)
**And** the rate-limit decision is logged with `event="router.rate_limited"` and the limit dimension that tripped

**Given** the queues and limits are in place
**When** a per-provider concurrency semaphore (`asyncio.Semaphore(4)` for `anthropic`; no semaphore for Ollama since it's local and the underlying server queues its own requests) gates the adapter dispatch
**Then** at most 4 Anthropic calls are in flight simultaneously regardless of lane priority
**And** waiting on the semaphore counts as "in flight" for `worker_health` purposes (does not stall other lane drains — the worker pool size of 8 absorbs the difference)

**Given** the system is under load (synthetic: 200 batch enqueues + 10 chat enqueues issued within 1 second in a test)
**When** the queues drain
**Then** every chat call completes before any batch call started after it (verifiable via timestamps on `router_calls`)
**And** chat-call p95 latency stays under 5 seconds for Qwen tasks (NFR-PERF-1) even with batch saturated
**And** no calls are dropped silently — every enqueue either returns a `RouterResult` or surfaces `rate_limited`

**Given** the worker pool is running
**When** the FastAPI app receives SIGTERM
**Then** the pool drains in-flight calls gracefully (with a configurable shutdown timeout, default 30 s) before exiting
**And** any unstarted queued calls return `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=PROVIDER_ERROR, message="shutdown", retryable=True))` to their callers

### Story 2.6: Anthropic adapter + Haiku + Opus + ephemeral prompt cache

As Adam,
I want an `AnthropicAdapter` that calls Claude Haiku 4.5 and Opus 4.7 with `cache_control: ephemeral` on the SYSTEM block on every call, with a 60-second hard timeout per FR-3.4, and full cache-hit accounting in `router_calls`,
So that Anthropic's prompt cache becomes a primary cost lever (Rule M) from day one and the system reports its own cache effectiveness.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 2.4 is complete and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is in `.env` accessed only via `get_secret(...)`
**When** `mailbot_api/router/models.py` is extended with `AnthropicAdapter`
**Then** ruff (per Story 1.4) permits `import anthropic` only inside `mailbot_api/router/models.py`
**And** the adapter exposes the same `async def call(system, user, max_tokens_out, temperature) -> AdapterResponse` interface as the Ollama adapter
**And** the adapter is instantiated for both `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` and `claude-opus-4-7` model IDs (sharing a single `anthropic.AsyncClient` per process)

**Given** the adapter is in use
**When** any call is dispatched
**Then** the request includes the SYSTEM block tagged with `cache_control={"type": "ephemeral"}` (per FR-3.6 and Rule M) — verifiable in `raw` payload
**And** the response's `usage.cache_read_input_tokens` and `usage.cache_creation_input_tokens` are surfaced as `cached_tokens_in` on `AdapterResponse`
**And** the call applies a 60-second hard timeout and raises `AdapterTimeout` on breach (FR-3.4 API-call timeout)

**Given** a Router call is dispatched against Haiku
**When** `record_router_call` writes the row
**Then** `cached_tokens_in` is populated correctly (zero on cold first call, non-zero on warm subsequent call within TTL)
**And** `cost_usd_estimated` reflects Anthropic's discounted rate for cached input tokens (looked up in `mailbot_api/router/pricing.py`)

**Given** `mailbot_api/router/pricing.py` is implemented
**When** a per-model price map is defined
**Then** it carries verified May-2026 token prices for `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` (input, cached-input, output) and `claude-opus-4-7` (input, cached-input, output)
**And** Ollama models have zero cost across the board (free-tier)
**And** `estimate_cost_usd(model, tokens_in, tokens_out, cached_tokens_in) -> float` is unit-tested for both providers across input/output/cached combinations

**Given** the Router calls Haiku twice in quick succession with identical SYSTEM blocks
**When** the second call returns
**Then** `cached_tokens_in > 0` (cache hit observable)
**And** the `router_calls` table can be queried to compute per-task cache hit rate via `SUM(cached_tokens_in) / SUM(tokens_in)` (raw SQL — no pandas)

**Given** the adapter is in place
**When** `tests/unit/router/test_anthropic_adapter.py` runs against a mocked Anthropic HTTP transport (httpx mock with recorded response fixtures)
**Then** the `cache_control` header is asserted present on every request
**And** cached-token accounting is verified across cold and warm fixture scenarios
**And** timeout behavior is covered

### Story 2.7: Response cache + cache warmer

As Adam,
I want a SQL-backed response cache keyed on `hash(prompt + model + temperature)` with per-task TTL configurable in `policy.yaml`, plus a cache warmer that re-pings high-volume tasks every 4 minutes to keep Anthropic's 5-minute prompt cache warm,
So that identical Router calls are free on repeat and high-volume tasks pay cached-input pricing instead of cold-call pricing.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 1.3's migration runner is in place
**When** migration `006_response_cache.sql` is added and runs at startup
**Then** the `response_cache` table is created with columns: `cache_key` (TEXT PRIMARY KEY — the hash), `task_type` (TEXT), `model` (TEXT), `result_json` (TEXT — serialized `RouterResult.output`), `cost_usd` (REAL), `cached_at` (TEXT, UTC ISO-8601), `ttl_seconds` (INTEGER), `hit_count` (INTEGER, default 0)
**And** an index `ix_response_cache_task_type_cached_at` is created for TTL sweep efficiency

**Given** `mailbot_api/router/response_cache.py` is implemented
**When** `ask_router` is invoked
**Then** the Router computes `cache_key = sha256(f"{model}|{temperature}|{system}|{user}").hexdigest()` and queries `response_cache` first
**And** if a row exists and `cached_at + ttl_seconds > now`, the cached `result_json` is returned as a `RouterResult` with `cost_usd=0`, `cached_tokens_in=0`, `model_used="<original>+response_cache"`, `outcome="ok"` (logged with `model_chosen_reason="response_cache_hit"`)
**And** `hit_count` is incremented on the row
**And** if no row exists or the TTL has expired, the dispatch proceeds normally and on success the result is written to `response_cache` with `ttl_seconds = policy.tasks[task_type].response_cache_ttl_seconds` (new field in `PolicyEntry`; default 0 = no caching)

**Given** `mailbot_api/router/cache_warmer.py` is implemented
**When** the worker process boots
**Then** the cache warmer registers itself as an asyncio interval task running every 4 minutes (aligned to Anthropic's 5-minute ephemeral TTL per Rule M)
**And** the warmer queries `policy.yaml` for tasks marked with `cache_warm: true` (new field in `PolicyEntry`; default false)
**And** for each warm-flagged task, the warmer issues a minimal probe call through `ask_router(task_type=<task>, content="<a small constant probe string>")` with `caller_origin="cache-warmer"` (FR-3.6)
**And** the warmer's calls are excluded from rate-limit accounting (rate limits in Story 2.5 ignore `caller_origin="cache-warmer"`)
**And** warmer failures log info-tier (`event="cache_warmer.failed"`) without retrying — best-effort per AR-PAT process patterns

**Given** the cache warmer has been running
**When** a production call for a warmed task dispatches against Anthropic
**Then** `cached_tokens_in > 0` (the warmer kept the SYSTEM block in Anthropic's cache)
**And** the per-task cache hit rate (computed from `router_calls`) trends above 80% for warmed tasks once steady state is reached

**Given** the response cache and warmer are in place
**When** the same call is issued twice within the response-cache TTL
**Then** the second call returns from `response_cache` (no adapter dispatch, no `router_calls` row that costs money — but still write a `router_calls` row with `outcome="ok"`, `cost_usd=0`, `model_chosen_reason="response_cache_hit"` for observability)

### Story 2.8: 4-layer budget guard + degraded mode + per-call refusal threshold

As Adam,
I want the 4-layer budget guard enforced inside the Router on every call: per-call `max_tokens_out` cap (Layer 1), $2 daily soft warn (Layer 2), $30 monthly hard cap entering degraded mode with demotion chain (Layer 3), and the $0.20 per-call refusal threshold (Layer 4) — plus the `/budget reset` slash command to manually exit degraded mode,
So that a single failure can't blow the $30/month cap and "agent stuffed the inbox into one prompt" bugs are refused before they hit the wire.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 2.4 is complete
**When** `mailbot_api/router/budget.py` is implemented
**Then** `BudgetGuard` is instantiated once at app startup and queried before every adapter dispatch
**And** the guard maintains an in-memory cache of `(today_spend_usd, this_month_spend_usd)` rolled forward from `router_calls` on startup
**And** each successful call updates the cache atomically inside the same `finally` block that writes the `router_calls` row

**Given** an `ask_router` call is in flight
**When** Layer 1 runs
**Then** the dispatched call's request specifies `max_tokens=policy.tasks[task_type].max_tokens_out` (default 4000 per FR-6.1)
**And** the adapter rejects any response that exceeds it (the provider truncates; our schema validation may or may not pass depending on the truncation point)

**Given** an `ask_router` call estimates `cost_usd > 0.20`
**When** Layer 4 runs (BEFORE dispatch, using `pricing.estimate_cost_usd` based on prompt size + max output tokens)
**Then** the call returns `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=PER_CALL_THRESHOLD_EXCEEDED, retryable=False, ...))` immediately
**And** the only override is the caller passing `force=true` (a new parameter on `ask_router`) — but `force=true` is logged with `model_chosen_reason="force_override"` for audit

**Given** today's cumulative spend has just crossed $2.00
**When** Layer 2 runs
**Then** a one-time chat notification is queued via `notifications.send_important("daily soft-warn: spent $X today; cap $30/mo")` (Epic 5 wires this to Discord; for now it writes to `notifications_pending` file per Story 1.8 stub)
**And** the threshold-crossed flag persists for the rest of the calendar day (resets at UTC midnight)
**And** subsequent calls today are NOT blocked (Layer 2 is warning only)

**Given** this month's cumulative spend has just crossed $30.00
**When** Layer 3 runs
**Then** the guard enters **degraded mode** (a single in-memory boolean persisted to a `degraded_mode_state` SQLite table — new migration `007_degraded_mode.sql` adds a single-row table with columns `active` (boolean), `entered_at` (text), `exited_at` (text nullable))
**And** an urgent chat notification fires once via `notifications.send_urgent(...)` with a message indicating the monthly cap was breached and degraded mode is active until `/budget reset` or next month rollover
**And** subsequent calls demote as follows: `claude-opus-4-7` → `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`; `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` → `qwen2.5:3b-instruct-q4_K_M`; Qwen stays Qwen (logged with `model_chosen_reason="degraded"`)
**And** any call with `force_model="claude-opus-4-7"` requires explicit chat confirmation: in degraded mode such a call returns `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=DEGRADED_MODE_BLOCKED, ...))` unless a fresh confirmation token (separate concept from sensitivity tokens — bound to `force_model_call_id` and an expiry timestamp) is provided. The token-mint flow for force-model-in-degraded-mode is deferred to Epic 5's chat surface; for now, the error path is what's tested
**And** degraded mode exits automatically at the next calendar-month rollover (UTC) OR when `/budget reset` is issued (this story implements the verb-side handler; Epic 5 wires the slash command UI)

**Given** the budget guard is in place
**When** `tests/unit/router/test_budget.py` runs
**Then** scenarios are covered: Layer 1 truncation; Layer 4 refusal at exactly $0.20 estimate; Layer 4 refusal with `force=true` allowed; Layer 2 single-fire warning across multiple calls in the same day; Layer 3 entry, demotion chain, force-Opus blocking, `/budget reset` clearing, month-rollover clearing

**Given** `/budget reset` handler is implemented in `mailbot_api/verbs/budget_admin.py`
**When** it is invoked
**Then** it flips the `degraded_mode_state` row to `(active=false, exited_at=<now>)`
**And** emits a structured log line `event="budget.degraded.exited"` with `reason="manual_reset"`
**And** returns a Pydantic `BudgetResetOut` model confirming the action

### Story 2.9: Hourly anomaly detection + anti-loop kill-switch + `/pause` / `/resume`

As Adam,
I want hourly Router call-volume anomaly detection (alerts before the budget breaches), prompt-hash repetition detection (refuses with `loop_detected` if the same prompt hash recurs > 10× in 5 minutes), and a `/pause` / `/resume` kill-switch that halts all Router calls without killing the FastAPI process,
So that runaway-agent loops are caught before they bankrupt me and I have an emergency stop that doesn't require SSH.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 2.1's `router_calls` table is in place
**When** `mailbot_api/router/limits.py` is extended with `LoopDetector`
**Then** the detector maintains a rolling 5-minute window of `(prompt_hash, count)` for hashes seen at least once
**And** when a hash crosses 10 occurrences in the window, subsequent `ask_router` calls with that hash return `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=LOOP_DETECTED, retryable=False, ...))` immediately
**And** the block lifts when the window slides past the offending occurrences
**And** loop-detected blocks are logged with `event="router.loop_detected"` including the (sanitized) prompt hash prefix

**Given** `mailbot_api/router/anomaly.py` is implemented
**When** the internal scheduler (Epic 6 builds the full scheduler; for now an asyncio interval task) runs hourly
**Then** the anomaly check computes the last hour's call volume per `caller_origin` from `router_calls`
**And** compares against a rolling 7-day per-hour baseline (stored in a `call_volume_baseline` table — new migration `008_anomaly_baseline.sql` adds it)
**And** when the current hour exceeds `baseline_mean + 3*baseline_stddev` for any `caller_origin`, fires a chat alert (`notifications.send_urgent("anomaly: <caller_origin> volume <X>x baseline this hour")`)
**And** baseline rows are upserted on each hourly tick (so the baseline evolves with real usage)
**And** the anomaly check is itself excluded from the rate-limit and budget systems (zero-cost SQL read)

**Given** `mailbot_api/router/__init__.py` exposes a module-level pause flag
**When** `/pause` is invoked (this story implements the verb-side handler `pause_router(reason: str)`; Epic 5 wires the slash command)
**Then** the flag is set; every subsequent `ask_router` call returns `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=PROVIDER_ERROR, message="router paused", retryable=True, ...))` immediately (does not enter the queue)
**And** `/resume` clears the flag and lets new calls dispatch normally
**And** in-flight calls at pause time are allowed to finish (no mid-call abort — we don't waste already-spent tokens)
**And** the pause state persists across worker process restarts (recorded in a single-row `pause_state` table via migration `009_pause_state.sql`)
**And** pause and resume both emit structured log lines

**Given** the anti-loop and pause mechanisms are in place
**When** `tests/unit/router/test_limits.py` runs
**Then** scenarios are covered: 11th identical prompt within 5 min returns `LOOP_DETECTED`; sliding window expiry releases the block; pause blocks new calls but lets in-flight finish; resume clears

### Story 2.10: `/cost` slash command + Hermes aux routing via `/v1/chat/completions` + `caller_origin` tracking

As Adam,
I want `POST /v1/chat/completions` exposed in OpenAI-compatible shape (so Hermes can configure it as a custom provider for its own auxiliary LLM work), the `caller_origin` column populated on every `router_calls` row, a `/cost` slash command returning today/month spend with per-task and per-model breakdowns, and a 24-hour drift alarm if zero Hermes-aux traffic is observed (signaling Hermes silently bypassed the Router),
So that Rule Ω extends to Hermes's own work and I can see at any moment exactly how my $30/month is being spent.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Stories 2.1–2.9 are complete
**When** `mailbot_api/main.py` exposes `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `POST /v1/embeddings`
**Then** the endpoint accepts an OpenAI-shape request body (`model`, `messages`, `max_tokens`, `temperature`, etc.) and an `Authorization: Bearer <MAILBOT_ROUTER_KEY>` header
**And** the endpoint validates the bearer matches `MAILBOT_ROUTER_KEY` from `.env` (returns `401` on mismatch)
**And** the endpoint translates the OpenAI-shape request to an internal `ask_router(task_type="hermes_aux", content=<messages>, force_model=<model>, ...)` invocation
**And** the response is translated back to OpenAI-shape (so Hermes treats us as a real provider)
**And** the `caller_origin` on the resulting `router_calls` row is set from a request header `X-Mailbot-Caller-Origin` (Hermes config provides `hermes-aux-compression` or `hermes-aux-title`; absent header defaults to `unknown-external`)

**Given** the audit table from Story 2.1
**When** any `ask_router` invocation runs
**Then** the `caller_origin` field is populated based on dispatch path: `verb-ask-router` (default, from the MCP-exposed verb in Epic 5), `cache-warmer` (from Story 2.7), `benchmark-runner` (Epic 7 will set this; until then, no benchmark runs), `hermes-aux-compression` / `hermes-aux-title` (from the `/v1/chat/completions` endpoint above)
**And** ad-hoc Python scripts invoking `ask_router` must pass `caller_origin="script:<script_name>"` explicitly (enforced by a default-to-`"unknown-internal"` and a startup ruff rule flagging any `ask_router(` call without an explicit `caller_origin=` kwarg — added to Story 1.4's rule set)

**Given** `mailbot_api/router/anomaly.py` from Story 2.9
**When** the hourly check runs and observes zero `caller_origin LIKE 'hermes-aux-%'` rows in `router_calls` for the last 24 hours
**Then** fires an informational chat alert `notifications.send_informational("Hermes aux drift: 0 calls in last 24h - check Hermes provider config")`
**And** the alert fires at most once per stale episode

**Given** `mailbot_api/verbs/cost.py` is implemented
**When** the `cost_breakdown(period: Literal["today", "month"]) -> CostBreakdownOut` verb is invoked
**Then** it returns a Pydantic model: `period`, `total_usd`, `cap_usd` (30.00 for month; absent for today), `per_task: dict[str, float]`, `per_model: dict[str, float]`, `per_caller_origin: dict[str, float]`, `cache_hit_rate: float` (computed as `sum(cached_tokens_in) / sum(tokens_in)` over the period), `call_count: int`, `degraded_mode_active: bool`
**And** all data is sourced from `router_calls` via raw SQL in `db/queries.py` (no pandas — AR-BOOT-2 deferral)

**Given** the verb is in place
**When** `/cost today` (handled by `mailbot_api/verbs/cost.py`; Epic 5 wires the slash command UI to call this verb) is invoked
**Then** the response renders as a chat-formatted block: today's $, today's call count, per-task top 5, per-model split, cache hit rate
**And** `/cost month` returns the same with the addition of `cap_usd=30` and `degraded_mode_active` rendered prominently

**Given** the endpoint and verb are in place
**When** `tests/integration/test_chat_completions_endpoint.py` runs against a live `mailbot-api` container with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set (opt-in test gated by env var)
**Then** posting a Hermes-shaped request returns an OpenAI-shape response
**And** the corresponding `router_calls` row has `caller_origin="hermes-aux-compression"` (per the header)
**And** invalid bearer returns 401 with OpenAI-shape error body (`{"error": {"type": "authentication_error", ...}}`)

**Given** the full Epic 2 stack is in place
**When** Adam manually runs through the smoke test
**Then** `make local` brings the stack up; curl `POST /v1/chat/completions` with the bearer succeeds; `router_calls` shows the row; `/cost today` (invoked via verb directly until Epic 5) returns the breakdown including this call

## Epic 3 Detail — Ingest Pipeline: Classify Once, Cached Forever

This epic operationalizes Rule A: every piece of LLM-derived information about an email is computed once at ingest and written back to the SQL row, never recomputed at chat time. The sensitivity-first hard invariant becomes provable: no Router call for any other task on `email_id` is permitted until `emails.sensitivity_at IS NOT NULL`. After this epic, Adam can run sync → ingest → query SQLite and see every email enriched with sensitivity, coarse class, fine class (where applicable), summary, importance score, action extraction, and embedding — with companion `*_prompt_v / _conf / _model / _at` columns proving provenance for every derived value.

### Story 3.1: Derived-field schema + companion metadata + idempotency helper

As Adam,
I want the `emails` table extended with all derived-field columns (sensitivity, coarse_class, fine_class, summary_short, importance_score, action_extraction, embedding) plus their companion `*_prompt_v / _conf / _model / _at` metadata, and a single `compute_idempotency_key()` helper centralizing the key formula,
So that every later story in this epic has a known schema to write into and a single place to read/compute idempotency from.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 1.3's migration runner is in place
**When** migration `011_derived_fields.sql` is added and runs at startup (renumbered +1 from epic-spec `010` — Epic 2's cumulative shift took `010_pause_state.sql`; see Epic 2 retro §6 "Required Epic 3 amendment")
**Then** the `emails` table gains derived-value columns: `sensitivity` (TEXT — one of `normal`/`sensitive`/`confidential`, nullable), `coarse_class` (TEXT, nullable), `fine_class` (TEXT, nullable), `summary_short` (TEXT, nullable), `importance_score` (INTEGER, nullable), `action_extraction` (TEXT — JSON-serialized action list, nullable), `embedding` (BLOB — little-endian float32 raw bytes per the W-5 resolution; nullable)
**And** for every derived value column `<X>`, four companion columns are added: `<X>_prompt_v` (TEXT, nullable), `<X>_conf` (REAL, nullable), `<X>_model` (TEXT, nullable), `<X>_at` (TEXT UTC ISO-8601 with `Z`, nullable)
**And** the `embedding` column gets TWO ADDITIONAL companion columns making the storage contract self-documenting: `embedding_dtype` (TEXT, nullable — e.g. `"<f4"` for little-endian float32) and `embedding_shape` (TEXT, nullable — JSON-encoded shape tuple, e.g. `"[768]"` for `nomic-embed-text`'s 768-dim output). This resolves Epic 1 retro action item #5 (W-5 embedding BLOB dtype/shape contract); decision recorded in Epic 2 retro post-decision §13 and `architecture.md` § AR-SCHEMA-2.
**And** indexes `ix_emails_sensitivity`, `ix_emails_coarse_class`, `ix_emails_importance_score`, and `ix_emails_sensitivity_at` are created (the last one enables fast "unprocessed queue" lookups for Story 3.6 backpressure)

**Given** `mailbot_api/ingest/idempotency.py` is implemented
**When** `compute_idempotency_key(body: str, prompt_version: str, model: str, task_type: str) -> str` is invoked
**Then** the result is `sha256(f"{body}|{prompt_version}|{model}|{task_type}").hexdigest()` (per FR-2.2 and Rule K)
**And** the helper is the only place this formula lives — any other module computing the key fails review

**Given** the schema and helper are in place
**When** `tests/unit/ingest/test_idempotency.py` runs
**Then** identical `(body, prompt_v, model, task)` inputs produce identical keys
**And** any single field change produces a different key
**And** the key is stable across Python interpreter invocations (no `hash()` randomization dependency)

**Given** the migration has applied
**When** `db.fetchone("SELECT sensitivity, sensitivity_prompt_v, sensitivity_conf, sensitivity_model, sensitivity_at FROM emails LIMIT 1")` is executed (with at least one synced row from Epic 1)
**Then** the query returns successfully with all columns present (all values may be NULL — derivation hasn't run yet)

### Story 3.2: Prompt modules for ingest tasks (uniform AR-PAT-5 structure)

As Adam,
I want the six ingest-task prompt modules (`sensitivity_class`, `coarse_class`, `fine_class`, `summary_short`, `importance_scoring`, `action_extraction`) written as `prompts/<task>/v1.py`, each exporting exactly `VERSION`, `SYSTEM`, `USER_TEMPLATE`, and `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` per AR-PAT-5,
So that the prompt-module structure is proven uniformly across six tasks before any of them runs in production, and the Router's prompt registry can load all of them by convention.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** AR-PAT-5 mandates exactly four exported names per prompt module
**When** each of `prompts/sensitivity_class/v1.py`, `prompts/coarse_class/v1.py`, `prompts/fine_class/v1.py`, `prompts/summary_short/v1.py`, `prompts/importance_scoring/v1.py`, `prompts/action_extraction/v1.py` is created
**Then** each module exports `VERSION` (str — e.g. `"v1"`), `SYSTEM` (str — cacheable; concise, defender-toned), `USER_TEMPLATE` (str — Python format-string with named placeholders), `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` (Pydantic v2 class)
**And** module-level `__all__ = ["VERSION", "SYSTEM", "USER_TEMPLATE", "OUTPUT_SCHEMA"]` declares the surface
**And** every prompt's `SYSTEM` block is written so it is shared verbatim across calls (Anthropic ephemeral cache benefit — Rule M) — i.e., no per-call interpolation in `SYSTEM`

**Given** `prompts/sensitivity_class/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires a single field `sensitivity: Literal["normal", "sensitive", "confidential"]` and `confidence: float` (range-validated 0..1) and `reason: str` (≤ 200 chars)

**Given** `prompts/coarse_class/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `class_coarse: Literal["transactional", "newsletter", "human", "notification", "spam_like", "unknown"]` and `confidence: float`

**Given** `prompts/fine_class/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `class_fine: Literal["personal", "professional", "family", "cold_outreach", "automated", "unknown"]` and `confidence: float`
**And** the SYSTEM block explicitly conditions the model to only consider this task when `class_coarse == "human"` (since Story 3.5 will only invoke `fine_class` after `coarse_class == "human"`)

**Given** `prompts/summary_short/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `summary: str` (≤ 280 chars — Twitter-length defender brevity)

**Given** `prompts/importance_scoring/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `importance: int` (range-validated 0..100) and `signals: list[str]` (≤ 5 short tags)

**Given** `prompts/action_extraction/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `actions: list[ActionItem]` where each `ActionItem` has `type: Literal["reply_needed", "deadline", "calendar_event", "payment", "password_reset", "info_only"]`, `summary: str` (≤ 120 chars), `deadline_at: str | None` (UTC ISO-8601 with `Z` when present)

**Given** `mailbot_api/prompts/__init__.py` is implemented
**When** the registry function `get_prompt(task_type: str, version: str) -> PromptModule` is called
**Then** it returns the loaded module by importing `prompts.<task_type>.<version>` dynamically and asserting all four exports are present
