**And** missing exports raise `PromptValidationError` at registry load time (startup), not at call time

**Given** the registry is in place
**When** `tests/unit/prompts/test_prompt_modules.py` parametrizes over all six task types
**Then** every module loads cleanly, every `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` instantiates against a valid sample, every `USER_TEMPLATE` accepts the documented placeholders without `KeyError`

### Story 3.3: Sensitivity classifier + `sensitivity_patterns.yaml` + Router precondition layer

As Adam,
I want the sensitivity classifier (Qwen-only, hard-coded — never escapes local LLM per FR-2.5), user-configurable forcing rules in `sensitivity_patterns.yaml`, and the Router precondition layer that refuses any non-sensitivity Router call on an `email_id` whose `sensitivity_at IS NULL` with `RouterError(code=SENSITIVITY_NOT_CLASSIFIED)`,
So that the FR-2.3 hard invariant is enforceable by construction and the "sensitive bodies never reach Anthropic" property is provable end-to-end.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 3.2's `prompts/sensitivity_class/v1.py` exists
**When** `mailbot_api/sensitivity/classifier.py` is implemented
**Then** `classify_sensitivity(email_id: str) -> SensitivityResult` reads the email body via `db.queries`, runs the classifier through `ask_router(task_type="sensitivity_class", content=<body>, email_id=email_id, caller_origin="ingest-pipeline")`
**And** the classifier wrapper hard-fails the call if `policy.tasks["sensitivity_class"].model` is anything other than the Qwen local model — refusing with a startup error if `policy.yaml` ever changes that assignment (FR-2.5 hard-coded enforcement, independent of policy table)
**And** the resulting sensitivity value, prompt_version, confidence, model, and current UTC timestamp are written to `emails.sensitivity`, `emails.sensitivity_prompt_v`, `emails.sensitivity_conf`, `emails.sensitivity_model`, and `emails.sensitivity_at` respectively

**Given** `mailbot_api/sensitivity/patterns.py` is implemented
**When** `router/sensitivity_patterns.yaml` is loaded at startup
**Then** the loader validates a Pydantic schema with three list fields: `force_confidential: list[Pattern]`, `force_sensitive: list[Pattern]`, where each `Pattern` is one of `{regex: str}`, `{sender_domain: str}`, or `{keyword: str}` (case-insensitive)
**And** `apply_pattern_override(email_id, classifier_result) -> SensitivityClass` runs after the classifier and force-promotes the result if any `force_confidential` rule matches (highest precedence), then any `force_sensitive` rule (downgrades from `confidential` are never applied)
**And** when an override fires, the override reason is captured in a new column `emails.sensitivity_override_reason` (TEXT, nullable — added by this story's migration `011_sensitivity_override.sql`)

**Given** the FR-2.3 hard invariant requires no other Router call on `email_id` until sensitivity is classified
**When** `mailbot_api/router/router.py` is extended with a precondition layer
**Then** every `ask_router(task_type=<X>, email_id=<id>)` invocation where `X != "sensitivity_class"` and `email_id is not None` first queries `emails.sensitivity_at` for `email_id`
**And** if `sensitivity_at IS NULL`, the call returns `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=SENSITIVITY_NOT_CLASSIFIED, retryable=False, message="email sensitivity must be classified before any other Router task"))` without dispatching
**And** if `sensitivity_at IS NOT NULL` and the call's task is API-bound (Haiku or Opus) and `emails.sensitivity IN ('sensitive', 'confidential')` and no valid `confirmation_token` is provided, the call refuses with `RouterError(code=SENSITIVITY_BLOCKS_API)` (the full token-mint handshake lands in Epic 4; this story stubs the refusal path)
**And** ad-hoc Router calls without an `email_id` (e.g., Hermes aux compression, cache warmer probes) bypass this precondition

**Given** the classifier and override pipeline are in place
**When** `tests/unit/sensitivity/test_classifier.py` and `tests/unit/sensitivity/test_patterns.py` run
**Then** scenarios are covered: clean classifier returning `normal`; classifier returning `sensitive` then pattern override promoting to `confidential`; regex pattern matching the subject line; sender_domain pattern matching `*@bank.com` (case-insensitive); confidence below 0.5 floors to `sensitive` per NFR-PRIV-1 cautious bias
**And** `tests/integration/test_sensitivity_precondition.py` verifies the Router refuses non-sensitivity calls on an unclassified `email_id` with `SENSITIVITY_NOT_CLASSIFIED` and accepts them after classification has run

**Given** the override mechanism is in place
**When** a `confidential`-overridden email is examined
**Then** any subsequent Router call with `force_model="claude-opus-4-7"` and a valid `confirmation_token` (Epic 4) is **still** refused — `confidential` admits no API override (FR-5.7, NFR-PRIV-2), tested by a stub token in this story

### Story 3.4: `nomic-embed-text` embedding adapter + embedding column writes

As Adam,
I want the Ollama adapter extended to call `nomic-embed-text` for embedding generation, and `mailbot_api/ingest/embedding.py` to serialize the embedding vector as a `float32` numpy array into `emails.embedding` (BLOB),
So that semantic search and similarity analysis over the mailbox become a primitive other stories can rely on.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the `ollama` container has `nomic-embed-text` pre-pulled per Story 2.3
**When** `OllamaAdapter` is extended with `async def embed(text: str) -> EmbeddingResponse` returning a Pydantic model `{vector: list[float], dim: int, tokens_in: int, latency_ms: int, raw: dict}`
**Then** the adapter dispatches via the `ollama` Python client's `embeddings` API against the `nomic-embed-text` model
**And** the returned `vector` length equals `dim` (768 for `nomic-embed-text`)
**And** the adapter timeout is 15 seconds (embeddings are faster than chat; 15 s catches stalls)

**Given** `mailbot_api/ingest/embedding.py` is implemented
**When** `write_embedding(email_id: str, vector: numpy.ndarray) -> None` is invoked
**Then** the function is the SOLE writer of `emails.embedding` + `emails.embedding_dtype` + `emails.embedding_shape` (W-5 resolution: single-writer monopoly, same pattern as `record_router_call` in Story 2-1)
**And** the vector is serialized via `vector.astype('<f4').tobytes()` (explicit little-endian float32 — guarantees cross-architecture portability per W-5 resolution)
**And** the row is written atomically with three companion values set together: `embedding` (BLOB), `embedding_dtype="<f4"` (TEXT), `embedding_shape=json.dumps(list(vector.shape))` (TEXT, e.g. `"[768]"`) — partial writes (any column NULL while others set) fail validation
**And** companion derived-field metadata is populated: `embedding_prompt_v="v1"` (sentinel — embeddings don't have prompt versions; "v1" identifies the embedding-model assignment row), `embedding_model="nomic-embed-text"`, `embedding_at=<now UTC>` — `embedding_conf` stays NULL (no confidence concept for embeddings)

**Given** `embed_email(email_id: str) -> None` is the higher-level orchestrator
**When** it is invoked
**Then** the function reads the email body via `db.queries`
**And** dispatches the embedding via `ask_router(task_type="embedding", content=<body>, email_id=email_id, caller_origin="ingest-pipeline")` — the Router routes embedding to the Ollama embed path (a small Router adjustment: when `policy.tasks["embedding"].model` resolves to an Ollama embedding model, dispatch via `adapter.embed(...)` not `adapter.call(...)`)
**And** calls `write_embedding(email_id, vector)` for persistence

**Given** the writer monopoly contract
**When** `scripts/check_boundaries.py` is extended with `_EMBEDDING_WRITE_ALLOW = frozenset({"mailbot_api/ingest/embedding.py"})` plus an AST scan for `UPDATE emails SET embedding` / `INSERT INTO emails (... embedding ...)` literals
**Then** any write to `emails.embedding` outside the allowlisted file fails the boundary check (same pattern as Story 2-1's `_ROUTER_CALLS_INSERT_ALLOW`)
**And** the AST walker covers `ast.JoinedStr` (f-string) nodes to prevent runtime-construction bypass (per Story 2-1 review finding R5)
**And** the boundary-fixture test placed in the allowlisted path passes clean (positive-pass coverage, per Story 2-1 review finding R8)

**Given** `mailbot_api/db/queries.py` exposes `get_embedding(email_id: str) -> numpy.ndarray | None`
**When** the function is invoked
**Then** it reads `emails.embedding` + `emails.embedding_dtype` + `emails.embedding_shape` and returns `numpy.frombuffer(blob, dtype=row.embedding_dtype).reshape(json.loads(row.embedding_shape))` if present, else `None` (reader uses the companion columns; never assumes a hard-coded dtype/shape)
**And** the returned array's shape is `(dim,)` (1D vector of length 768 for `nomic-embed-text`)

**Given** the embedding pipeline is in place
**When** `tests/unit/ingest/test_embedding.py` runs against a mocked Ollama embed response
**Then** the round-trip (vector → bytes → numpy array) preserves all 768 values without precision loss beyond `float32`
**And** the serialized blob size is exactly `768 * 4 = 3072` bytes per row
**And** the cross-architecture portability test asserts the written bytes match `numpy.asarray([...], dtype='<f4').tobytes()` byte-for-byte (catches accidental big-endian dtype or native-byte-order writes)

**Given** a seeded email row
**When** `embed_email(email_id)` is invoked and then `get_embedding(email_id)` retrieves the vector
**Then** computing `numpy.linalg.norm(vector)` returns a positive finite value (embedding is not all zeros)
**And** computing cosine similarity between two seeded embeddings of similar content returns a value > 0.7; for unrelated content < 0.3 (smoke test of the embedding model's basic discriminative behavior)

### Story 3.5: Pipeline ordering enforcement (single-email end-to-end)

As Adam,
I want `mailbot_api/ingest/pipeline.py` to orchestrate the fixed task ordering `sensitivity_class → coarse_class → (fine_class if class_coarse == "human") → summary_short → importance_scoring → action_extraction → embedding` per FR-2.3, writing each derived value plus companion metadata to the email row in order,
So that a single email can be processed end-to-end and the Rule A contract ("derived fields computed once, cached forever") becomes provable for an arbitrary inbox row.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Stories 3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.4 are complete
**When** `mailbot_api/ingest/pipeline.py` is implemented
**Then** `async process_email(email_id: str) -> ProcessEmailResult` runs the fixed ordering above, calling `classify_sensitivity → ask_router(coarse_class) → (conditionally ask_router(fine_class)) → ask_router(summary_short) → ask_router(importance_scoring) → ask_router(action_extraction) → embed_email`
**And** each successful step writes (`<task>`, `<task>_prompt_v`, `<task>_conf`, `<task>_model`, `<task>_at`) atomically per step (one `execute_write` per step — no batch-update games)
**And** each step's idempotency is checked via `compute_idempotency_key` before dispatching: if a row in a new `derivations_idempotency` table (migration `012_derivations_idempotency.sql` — columns: `email_id`, `task_type`, `idempotency_key`, `applied_at`) already records the same key for this `(email_id, task_type)`, the step is skipped (FR-2.2 — re-runs are free)
**And** any step returning `ok=False` aborts the pipeline for this email; the email row carries partial derivation (whatever finished before the failure); a structured log line `event="ingest.step.failed"` is emitted with the email_id, task_type, and error code

**Given** a single seeded email of `class_coarse == "human"` content
**When** `process_email(email_id)` runs
**Then** all 7 steps execute in order (verifiable via `router_calls` row timestamps for this `email_id`)
**And** `emails.sensitivity_at`, `coarse_class_at`, `fine_class_at`, `summary_short_at`, `importance_score_at`, `action_extraction_at`, `embedding_at` are all populated and strictly monotonically increasing (sensitivity first per FR-2.3)
**And** all companion fields (`*_prompt_v`, `*_conf`, `*_model`) are populated per step

**Given** a single seeded email of `class_coarse == "newsletter"`
**When** `process_email(email_id)` runs
**Then** `fine_class` is **not** invoked (the conditional gate skips it) — `emails.fine_class_at IS NULL` and no `router_calls` row exists for `(task_type="fine_class", email_id=this)`
**And** the remaining steps complete normally

**Given** an email that already has all derivations from a prior run
**When** `process_email(email_id)` is invoked again at the same prompt versions
**Then** all 7 steps short-circuit via idempotency check
**And** zero new `router_calls` rows are written
**And** the function returns within < 100 ms total

**Given** the sensitivity step fails (Qwen unreachable)
**When** `process_email(email_id)` runs
**Then** subsequent steps do not execute (the FR-2.3 hard invariant catches them via the Router precondition layer, but the pipeline orchestrator already short-circuits to avoid the wasted dispatch)
**And** `emails.sensitivity_at IS NULL`, all later `*_at` fields also NULL

**Given** a sensitive-classified email
**When** `process_email(email_id)` runs the steps that route to Anthropic
**Then** every Anthropic-bound step refuses with `SENSITIVITY_BLOCKS_API` (no confirmation token at ingest time)
**And** Qwen-routed steps proceed normally
**And** the email row ends with all Qwen-derived fields populated, Anthropic-bound fields NULL (and log lines explain why — `event="ingest.step.skipped_sensitive"`)

**Given** the single-email pipeline is in place and a fresh sync has pulled at least one row from real Outlook
**When** `python -m mailbot_api.ingest.pipeline --email-id <id>` is invoked from the dev shell
**Then** the row is fully enriched end-to-end and the total wall-clock time is < 30 s on the 2-vCPU VPS (NFR-PERF-3 — per-step p95 ≤ 5 s for Qwen tasks; 7 sequential steps + small overhead)

### Story 3.6: Backpressure ceiling + batched processing (bulk pipeline drain)

As Adam,
I want the ingest pipeline to drain unprocessed emails in 100-batch chunks via the batch lane (FR-2.4, NFR-PERF-4), with backpressure logic that throttles deeper if `unprocessed_count > 500` to keep the interactive lane responsive,
So that a first-time sync of a 10,000-email inbox processes in the background without ever blocking a chat query.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 3.5's `process_email` is in place
**When** `mailbot_api/ingest/backpressure.py` and `mailbot_api/ingest/pipeline.py`'s `run_batch()` are implemented
**Then** `run_batch()` queries `emails WHERE sensitivity_at IS NULL ORDER BY received_at DESC LIMIT 100` to select the next 100 unprocessed
**And** processes them via `process_email(email_id)` one at a time (sequential within the batch — concurrency comes from the Router's worker pool dispatching Qwen calls)
**And** all dispatches use the `batch` lane (`caller_origin="ingest-pipeline"`)
**And** after the batch completes, the unprocessed count is re-queried; if > 500, the function sleeps 5 s before allowing the next batch (deeper backpressure); if ≤ 500, the next batch runs immediately

**Given** the `mailbot-api` worker process is running
**When** the internal scheduler (Epic 6 builds the full scheduler; this story adds a stub interval task that runs every 5 minutes) ticks
**Then** `run_batch()` is invoked
**And** the worker writes a `worker_health` row with `(component="ingest_pipeline", last_heartbeat_at, last_outcome, last_error)` per Story 1.8's pattern
**And** repeated empty queues result in `last_outcome="ok"` with `messages_processed=0`

**Given** Story 2.5's lane priority is in place
**When** an ingest batch is in flight (100 emails draining through Qwen) and a chat query arrives via `/v1/chat/completions` with `caller_origin="hermes-aux-compression"`
**Then** the chat call's dispatch preempts further batch dequeue (only one batch email completes; the next batch slot waits)
**And** the chat call's p95 latency stays under 5 s (NFR-PERF-1) — verifiable by a load test with a synthetic 500-email backlog

**Given** the backpressure logic is in place
**When** `tests/integration/test_ingest_backpressure.py` seeds 600 unprocessed emails and triggers the scheduler
**Then** the pipeline drains 100 at a time
**And** after every 100, the unprocessed-count check triggers the 5 s sleep (since count stays > 500 for the first batches)
**And** after the 5th batch (count now ≤ 500), the throttle releases
**And** total processing time scales linearly with no batch-to-batch lag accumulation

**Given** a sensitive-classified email is in the batch
**When** `process_email` runs
**Then** all Qwen-derived steps complete normally (no `SENSITIVITY_BLOCKS_API` for Qwen tasks)
**And** any Haiku/Opus-bound steps return `RouterResult(ok=False, error=...SENSITIVITY_BLOCKS_API)` and the orchestrator records that step as "skipped at ingest" without aborting the rest of the pipeline (Adam can later confirm via chat to mint a token and re-derive via Story 3.8)

### Story 3.7: Senders + threads upsert + cached `sender_reputation_summary`

As Adam,
I want every new email's sender to upsert a `senders` row, and on first contact to generate a one-line `sender_reputation_summary` via Qwen and cache it forever (Rule A); threads similarly upserted with a cached `thread_continuity_note` populated on first multi-message thread detection,
So that chat-time queries like "anything from the lawyer" can read the cached one-liner without re-paying for synthesis on every lookup.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 1.7 created `senders` and `threads` rows via sync upsert (basic columns: `sender_id`, `address`, `display_name`, `first_seen_at`, `last_seen_at`, `message_count`)
**When** migration `013_sender_reputation.sql` and `014_thread_continuity.sql` are added and run at startup
**Then** the `senders` table gains: `sender_reputation_summary` (TEXT, nullable), `sender_reputation_summary_prompt_v` (TEXT, nullable), `sender_reputation_summary_model` (TEXT, nullable), `sender_reputation_summary_at` (TEXT, nullable)
**And** the `threads` table gains: `thread_continuity_note` (TEXT, nullable) plus the same `*_prompt_v / _model / _at` companions

**Given** `prompts/sender_reputation_summary/v1.py` is added per AR-PAT-5
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `summary: str` (≤ 140 chars — single defender-toned line)
**And** the `USER_TEMPLATE` accepts a placeholder for "the most recent 5 emails from this sender" (Adam's recent context for synthesis)

**Given** `mailbot_api/ingest/sender_enrichment.py` is implemented
**When** a new sender is detected (Story 1.7's upsert just inserted the row — `senders.sender_reputation_summary IS NULL`)
**Then** `enrich_sender(sender_id)` is invoked from the ingest pipeline orchestrator AFTER all per-email derivations for that batch complete
**And** the function queries the 5 most recent emails from this sender via `db.queries`
**And** dispatches `ask_router(task_type="sender_reputation_summary", content=<5-email digest>, email_id=None, caller_origin="ingest-pipeline-sender")` (note: `email_id=None` bypasses the FR-2.3 sensitivity precondition — sender summaries are not per-email tasks)
**And** writes the result to `senders.sender_reputation_summary` and companion metadata

**Given** a sender whose `sender_reputation_summary` is already populated
**When** a new email arrives from that sender
**Then** `enrich_sender(sender_id)` short-circuits (returns immediately without dispatching) — Rule A: cached forever
**And** the cached value is unchanged

**Given** a thread now has > 1 message (i.e. became a real thread)
**When** `enrich_thread(thread_id)` is invoked after the ingest batch
**Then** if `threads.thread_continuity_note IS NULL`, dispatch `ask_router(task_type="thread_continuity", content=<thread digest>, email_id=None, caller_origin="ingest-pipeline-thread")` and write the result
**And** if already populated, short-circuit (Rule A)
**And** `prompts/thread_continuity/v1.py` exists per AR-PAT-5 with `summary: str` (≤ 200 chars)

**Given** sender and thread enrichment are in place
**When** `tests/integration/test_sender_thread_enrichment.py` seeds two emails from a new sender and a 3-message thread
**Then** the first email's batch triggers sender summary generation
**And** the second email's batch does NOT trigger another summary generation
**And** the `senders.sender_reputation_summary` and `threads.thread_continuity_note` are populated with non-empty strings

**Given** any sensitive emails are present in a sender's recent-5 window
**When** `enrich_sender` is invoked
**Then** the digest passed to the Router excludes the body of sensitive/confidential emails (only metadata: subject, received_at) — preserving FR-2.5's "sensitive bodies stay local" property across the cross-email synthesis boundary
**And** `confidential` emails are excluded entirely (not even metadata)

### Story 3.8: Targeted re-derivation CLI

As Adam,
I want `mailbot rederive --task=<task> --since=<date> [--prompt-v=<version>]` to opt-in re-run a single ingest task on selected rows when I bump a prompt version or want to re-evaluate after a user-model refresh,
So that calibration-driven re-derivation (FR-2.6) is a deliberate, scoped, evidence-backed operation — never accidental.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `scripts/mailbot` (placeholder CLI from Story 1.8) is extended
**When** `mailbot rederive --task=coarse_class --since=2026-04-01` is invoked
**Then** the CLI looks up `policy.tasks["coarse_class"].prompt_version` (or uses `--prompt-v` if passed) and lists every `emails` row where `received_at >= 2026-04-01` and `(coarse_class_at IS NULL OR coarse_class_prompt_v != <target version>)`
**And** prints the count of rows that will be re-derived, the estimated cost (Qwen = $0.00; if the task routes to Anthropic, computed via `pricing.estimate_cost_usd`), and the estimated wall-clock time
**And** asks for confirmation: `Proceed? [y/N]`
**And** on `n` or no input within 30 s, exits with no changes
**And** on `y`, dispatches `ask_router(task_type="coarse_class", ...)` for each row, writes results to the derived column + companion fields, updates `derivations_idempotency` with the new key

**Given** the re-derivation runs
**When** the per-row dispatch happens
**Then** each call uses `caller_origin="cli-rederive"` (a new value added to the `caller_origin` set in Story 2.10)
**And** progress logs every 50 rows
**And** Ctrl+C aborts cleanly (rows already re-derived remain re-derived; pending rows untouched; no partial-row updates)

**Given** the user passes a task that requires the sensitivity precondition
**When** any selected row has `sensitivity_at IS NULL`
**Then** the CLI refuses to start, printing "{count} rows are unclassified for sensitivity — re-derivation requires sensitivity to have run first"
**And** exits non-zero with no Router dispatches

**Given** the user passes `--task=sensitivity_class --since=<date>` (re-deriving sensitivity itself)
**When** the CLI runs
**Then** for each row, the existing sensitivity value AND all downstream derived fields are cleared (set to NULL, companion fields cleared) before sensitivity is re-derived — because downstream values may no longer be valid under a new sensitivity classification
**And** the CLI prints a prominent warning before confirmation: "Re-deriving sensitivity will clear all downstream derivations for {count} rows. Continue? [y/N]"

**Given** the CLI is in place
**When** `tests/integration/test_rederive_cli.py` exercises three scenarios
**Then** scenario A: re-deriving `coarse_class` for 10 seeded rows succeeds, idempotency table is updated, no downstream column is touched
**And** scenario B: re-deriving `sensitivity_class` clears downstream fields first
**And** scenario C: aborting at the confirmation prompt leaves all rows unchanged

---

## Epic 4 Detail — Authorized Actions: Adam Approves Every Send and Delete

This epic makes the 4-tier authorization model **verb-API enforced** rather than agent-trusted: the agent cannot promote an action's tier, the drainer performs a second auth check at drain time, the 20-send/day cap is unconditional, and the sensitive-content API override handshake gives every sensitive-to-API call a clean mint/consume audit pair. Outlook write-back ships with the hybrid sync conflict policy (Tier-3 ETag-strict + Tier-1/2 lenient 3-rule) and the error-classified Graph retry chain. After this epic, Adam can grant in chat, see actions accumulate in `pending_actions`, watch the drainer apply them safely, and revert any Tier-1 action within 24 hours.

### Story 4.1: `ActionType` enum + `tier_for()` + cross-cutting properties table

As Adam,
I want a single `mailbot_api/actions/types.py` module declaring all 19 action types as a Python `Enum` with their tier mapping, reversibility window, change-marker requirement, send-budget membership, and sensitivity-token requirement,
So that every later story in this epic — and every future verb that handles actions — reads from one source of truth, and string-literal action_types anywhere else in the codebase fail review.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `mailbot_api/actions/types.py` is implemented
**When** the module is imported
**Then** an `ActionType` `Enum` is defined with these 19 members (snake_case values): Tier 0 — `READ_SQL`, `ASK_ROUTER`, `GENERATE_DRAFT`, `SEND_CHAT_NOTIFICATION`, `WRITE_DERIVED_FIELD`; Tier 1 — `MARK_READ`, `MARK_UNREAD`, `ADD_LOCAL_CATEGORY`, `REMOVE_LOCAL_CATEGORY`, `MOVE_TO_TRIAGE_FOLDER`; Tier 2 — `ARCHIVE`, `MARK_JUNK`, `MOVE_TO_USER_FOLDER`, `UNSUBSCRIBE`, `MOVE_TO_INBOX`; Tier 3 — `DELETE`, `SEND_REPLY`, `SEND_NEW_EMAIL`, `SEND_FORWARD`, `REPLY_TO_INACTIVE_THREAD`, `MODIFY_INBOX_RULE`, `MODIFY_OUTLOOK_FILTER`, `TOUCH_DELEGATED_MAILBOX`
**And** each enum member's `value` is the snake_case string (e.g. `ActionType.MARK_READ.value == "mark_read"`)

**Given** a frozen `ACTION_PROPERTIES: dict[ActionType, ActionProperties]` is defined in the same module
**When** any `ActionType` is looked up
**Then** `ActionProperties` is a Pydantic v2 model with fields: `tier: Literal[0, 1, 2, 3]`, `reversibility_window_hours: int | None` (24 for Tier 1, `None` otherwise), `change_marker_required: bool` (True only for Tier 3), `budget_against: Literal["daily_send_cap_20"] | None` (set on `SEND_REPLY`, `SEND_NEW_EMAIL`, `SEND_FORWARD`, `REPLY_TO_INACTIVE_THREAD`; `None` otherwise), `requires_sensitivity_token: bool` (True for any Tier-2/3 action generating outbound content from a `sensitive`-classified email — practically: True for the four `SEND_*` actions and `REPLY_TO_INACTIVE_THREAD`; False otherwise)

**Given** the registry is in place
**When** `tier_for(action_type: ActionType) -> int` is called
**Then** it returns `ACTION_PROPERTIES[action_type].tier`
**And** `tier_for(ActionType.MARK_READ) == 1`
**And** `tier_for(ActionType.DELETE) == 3`
**And** `tier_for(ActionType.SEND_REPLY) == 3`

**Given** Story 1.4's ruff rule set
**When** the rule set is extended (per this story)
**Then** any string literal whose value is in `{at.value for at in ActionType}` appearing outside `mailbot_api/actions/types.py` or `tests/` fails the lint pass
**And** a deliberately-violating fixture (e.g. `propose_action("delete", ...)` with a raw string) triggers the rule when added to the lint root

**Given** `tests/unit/actions/test_types.py` is implemented
**When** the test exercises every enum member
**Then** every `ActionType` has a complete `ActionProperties` entry (no missing entries)
**And** the four `SEND_*`-family actions all share `budget_against="daily_send_cap_20"` and `requires_sensitivity_token=True`
**And** all Tier-1 actions have `reversibility_window_hours=24`; all Tier-2/3 actions have `reversibility_window_hours=None`
**And** only Tier-3 actions have `change_marker_required=True`

### Story 4.2: `pending_actions` + `action_grants` + `action_history` schema + `propose_action` verb

As Adam,
I want the three queue tables created via migrations and the `propose_action(email_id, action_type, **payload) → ProposeActionOut` verb that consults `tier_for()` at insert, captures the current `emails.change_marker`, and routes the row to the correct initial status (`cooling_off` for Tier-3 sends, `pending_grant` for Tier-2 batches, `pending` for Tier-1 auto-actions),
So that the agent's first interaction with the action system is structurally tier-aware and the agent cannot promote an action's tier.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 1.3's migration runner is in place
**When** migrations `015_pending_actions.sql`, `016_action_grants.sql`, and `017_action_history.sql` are added and run at startup
**Then** `pending_actions` is created with columns: `id` (INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT), `email_id` (TEXT, nullable for actions like `MODIFY_INBOX_RULE` that aren't email-scoped), `action_type` (TEXT — validated against `ActionType` enum at insert), `tier` (INTEGER — derived from `tier_for(action_type)` at insert), `payload` (TEXT — JSON-serialized verb payload), `proposed_at` (TEXT UTC ISO-8601 with `Z`), `proposed_by_grant_id` (INTEGER, nullable — FK to `action_grants.id` for Tier-2 actions covered by a batch grant), `change_marker_at_propose` (TEXT, nullable — captured for Tier-3 ETag check at drain), `status` (TEXT — one of `pending`/`cooling_off`/`pending_grant`/`draining`/`applied`/`failed`/`cancelled`), `retry_count` (INTEGER default 0), `failure_reason` (TEXT, nullable), `terminal_at` (TEXT, nullable), `budget_consumed` (INTEGER — 0/1 boolean, default 0)
**And** indexes `ix_pending_actions_status_proposed_at`, `ix_pending_actions_email_id`, and `ix_pending_actions_action_type` are created
**And** `action_grants` is created with columns: `id` (INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT), `action_type` (TEXT), `email_ids` (TEXT — JSON array), `expires_at` (TEXT UTC ISO-8601), `minted_at` (TEXT UTC ISO-8601), `revoked_at` (TEXT, nullable)
**And** `action_history` is created with columns: `action_id` (INTEGER PRIMARY KEY — FK to `pending_actions.id`), `pre_state` (TEXT — JSON snapshot), `applied_at` (TEXT UTC ISO-8601), `reverted_at` (TEXT, nullable)

**Given** `mailbot_api/verbs/propose_action.py` is implemented
**When** `propose_action(email_id, action_type, **payload) -> ProposeActionOut` is called via MCP
**Then** the verb validates `action_type` against `ActionType` (rejects unknown values with `ProposeActionOut(ok=False, error=...)` — never raises)
**And** rejects any payload containing a `tier` field with `ProposeActionOut(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=PROVIDER_ERROR, message="tier is computed by the verb API and cannot be agent-specified"))` — this is FR-5.6 enforced at the verb boundary
**And** computes `tier = tier_for(action_type)`
**And** if `tier == 3` and `action_type` is in the `SEND_*` family, captures the current `emails.change_marker` for `email_id` and inserts as `status="cooling_off"` with `change_marker_at_propose=<value>`
**And** if `tier == 3` and `action_type == ActionType.DELETE`, captures `change_marker` and inserts as `status="pending"` (no cooling-off for deletes — they're already grant-gated)
**And** if `tier == 2`, inserts as `status="pending_grant"` with `change_marker_at_propose=NULL` (lenient policy per AR-D4-2 applies at drain time, not propose time)
**And** if `tier == 1`, inserts as `status="pending"` with `change_marker_at_propose=NULL`
**And** returns `ProposeActionOut(ok=True, action_id=<id>, tier=<tier>, status=<initial_status>, error=None)`

**Given** the verb is in place and ruff (from Story 4.1) blocks string-literal action types outside `types.py`
**When** any caller invokes `propose_action`
**Then** they pass `action_type=ActionType.MARK_READ` (or another enum member) — passing a bare string is a lint failure caught before runtime

**Given** the `propose_action` verb writes a row
**When** the structured log line fires
**Then** `event="action.proposed"` is emitted with `action_id`, `action_type`, `tier`, `status`, `email_id`
**And** sensitive payload fields (e.g. `body` content in send actions) are not logged

**Given** `tests/unit/actions/test_propose_action.py` is implemented
**When** the test exercises one action of each tier
**Then** Tier-0 actions are refused at the verb boundary with `ProposeActionOut(ok=False, error=...)` and message "Tier 0 actions are verb-level capabilities, not user-visible actions — they do not enter pending_actions" (Tier 0 doesn't queue)
**And** Tier-1 inserts with `status="pending"`, no change_marker captured
**And** Tier-2 inserts with `status="pending_grant"`, no change_marker captured
**And** Tier-3 SEND_REPLY inserts with `status="cooling_off"`, change_marker captured
**And** Tier-3 DELETE inserts with `status="pending"`, change_marker captured
**And** an attempted tier-override payload is refused with the documented error message

### Story 4.3: Scoped time-bounded grants + `mint_grant` verb + `is_grant_valid` helper

As Adam,
I want a `mint_grant(action_type, email_ids, expires_at) → MintGrantOut` Tier-0 verb that writes a row to `action_grants`, and an `is_grant_valid(action_type, email_id) → bool` helper that the drainer (Story 4.4) calls for its second auth check at drain time,
So that I can grant "you may delete the 47 emails I just identified" in chat with a 1-hour window, and the system enforces the grant scope and expiry by construction.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `mailbot_api/verbs/mint_grant.py` and `mailbot_api/actions/authorization.py` are implemented
**When** `mint_grant(action_type: ActionType, email_ids: list[str], expires_at: datetime) -> MintGrantOut` is called via MCP
**Then** the verb validates `expires_at` is strictly in the future (rejects past timestamps with `MintGrantOut(ok=False, error=...)`)
**And** validates `expires_at` is at most 24 hours from now (rejects longer grants with the same error shape — defender bias: grants are short by design)
**And** validates `len(email_ids) <= 200` (caps batch grant size — bulk operations beyond this require a fresh grant; prevents one-grant-rules-them-all anti-pattern)
**And** inserts a row into `action_grants` with `(action_type=<value>, email_ids=<JSON array>, expires_at=<UTC ISO-8601 Z>, minted_at=<now>, revoked_at=NULL)`
**And** returns `MintGrantOut(ok=True, grant_id=<id>, expires_at=<value>, email_count=<n>)`

**Given** `is_grant_valid(action_type: ActionType, email_id: str | None) -> tuple[bool, int | None]` is implemented in `mailbot_api/actions/authorization.py`
**When** the function is called
**Then** it returns `(True, grant_id)` if there exists at least one row in `action_grants` where `action_type == <value>` AND `email_id IN (parsed email_ids JSON)` AND `expires_at > now` AND `revoked_at IS NULL`
**And** returns `(False, None)` otherwise
**And** Tier-1 actions (which require no grant) are not the concern of this helper — the drainer (Story 4.4) only calls it for Tier-2/3 rows

**Given** a grant has been minted for `(ActionType.DELETE, [email_id_A, email_id_B], expires_at=<+1h>)`
**When** `is_grant_valid(ActionType.DELETE, email_id_A)` is called
**Then** the result is `(True, grant_id)`
**And** `is_grant_valid(ActionType.DELETE, email_id_C)` (not in the grant's email_ids) returns `(False, None)`
**And** `is_grant_valid(ActionType.SEND_REPLY, email_id_A)` (right email, wrong action) returns `(False, None)`

**Given** a grant exists and has expired
**When** `is_grant_valid` is called
**Then** the result is `(False, None)`
**And** the expired row is NOT auto-deleted (audit trail preserved) — a daily cleanup job (Epic 6 cron) sweeps `action_grants WHERE expires_at < now() - INTERVAL '30 days'`

**Given** `revoke_grant(grant_id: int) → RevokeGrantOut` is implemented
**When** the verb is called
**Then** the row's `revoked_at` is set to `now()`
**And** subsequent `is_grant_valid` calls covered by this grant return `(False, None)`
**And** the structured log line `event="action.grant.revoked"` is emitted

**Given** `tests/unit/actions/test_authorization.py` is implemented
**When** it exercises mint + validate + revoke + expiry scenarios
**Then** all paths are covered: successful mint; rejection of past `expires_at`; rejection of > 24h grant; rejection of > 200 email_ids; validation success and failure across action_type / email_id / expiry / revoked dimensions

### Story 4.4: Drainer with second auth check + Tier-3 ETag + lenient Tier-1/2 conflict policy

As Adam,
I want `mailbot_api/actions/drainer.py` to run as a continuous loop in the worker process, pick up `pending_actions` rows in priority order (Tier-1 fastest, Tier-2/3 with their auth + ETag checks), and apply the hybrid sync conflict policy (Tier-3 strict ETag match per AR-D4-1, Tier-1/2 lenient 3 rules per AR-D4-2),
So that "agent decides to skip a check" is structurally impossible — every drained action passes the auth and consistency gates before reaching Outlook.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 4.2's schema + Story 4.3's authorization helper are in place
**When** `mailbot_api/actions/drainer.py` is implemented and registered with the worker process from Story 1.8
**Then** the drainer runs as a continuous asyncio loop (not cron — per AR-D13-1 "pending_actions drainer continuous loop, not cron")
**And** on every tick (default poll interval 2s, configurable), the drainer queries `SELECT * FROM pending_actions WHERE status = 'pending' ORDER BY tier ASC, proposed_at ASC LIMIT 25`
**And** for each row, sets `status="draining"` first (single UPDATE — claims the row; prevents double-processing if two drainers ever run)
**And** then performs the per-tier checks below before dispatching to Graph (Story 4.5)

**Given** a Tier-1 row is being drained
**When** the checks run
**Then** no grant check is needed (Tier-1 is auto-revertible per FR-5.1)
**And** the lenient 3-rule conflict policy from AR-D4-2 is applied: (rule 1) if `emails.deleted_at IS NOT NULL` for `email_id`, mark row `status="failed"` with `failure_reason="target_deleted"`, silent log, no urgent notification (Tier-1 silent log per AR-D5-4); (rule 2) if target moved but exists, execute the action (idempotent actions like `mark_read` are still valid); (rule 3) if target moved AND action is a folder-move (`MOVE_TO_TRIAGE_FOLDER`), re-resolve target folder; execute if still semantically valid, else mark `status="failed"` with `failure_reason="state_drift_noop"`

**Given** a Tier-2 row is being drained
**When** the checks run
**Then** the drainer calls `is_grant_valid(action_type, email_id)` and refuses to drain (leaves `status="pending_grant"`) if no valid grant exists — re-queries on next tick (waits for grant)
**And** the lenient 3-rule conflict policy from AR-D4-2 applies (same as Tier-1)
**And** Tier-2 failures notify in the next daily digest per AR-D5-4 — for now, the drainer writes the failure to a `notifications_pending` file with category `important` (Epic 6 wires the digest)

**Given** a Tier-3 row is being drained
**When** the checks run
**Then** the drainer calls `is_grant_valid(action_type, email_id)` and refuses to drain if no valid grant exists; if no grant arrives within 30 minutes of `proposed_at`, marks `status="failed"` with `failure_reason="grant_expired_unauthorized"`, urgent notification fires (per AR-D5-4)
**And** the **strict ETag check** runs: compares `pending_actions.change_marker_at_propose` against the current `emails.change_marker` (latest sync value); if they differ, marks `status="failed"` with `failure_reason="state_drift_etag"`, urgent notification fires, returns `RouterError(code=STATE_DRIFT_ETAG)` for any caller observing the row
**And** if `emails.deleted_at IS NOT NULL`, marks `status="failed"` with `failure_reason="target_deleted"`, urgent notification fires (per AR-D5-4 Tier-3 urgent)
**And** only on all checks passing does the drainer proceed to Story 4.5's `apply_action_to_graph`

**Given** an action is about to be dispatched to Graph
**When** the dispatch happens (Story 4.5 implements the actual Graph call; this story stubs it with a fake adapter that returns success)
**Then** the drainer writes a pre-state snapshot to `action_history(action_id, pre_state, applied_at, reverted_at=NULL)` BEFORE the Graph call (so a partial failure can still be audited)
**And** on Graph success, the drainer sets `status="applied"`, `terminal_at=<now>`, and `budget_consumed=true` (if `action_type` has `budget_against="daily_send_cap_20"`)
**And** on Graph failure (Story 4.5 handles the retry chain — this story trusts its return value), the drainer sets `status="failed"`, `failure_reason=<sanitized message>`, `terminal_at=<now>`, and `budget_consumed=true` for send-family actions (AR-D5-2 — failed sends consume budget too)

**Given** the drainer is in place
**When** `tests/unit/actions/test_drainer.py` exercises one row per tier through both happy-path and conflict-resolution scenarios
**Then** Tier-1 silent log on `target_deleted`; Tier-2 `pending_grant` until grant arrives; Tier-3 refusal on missing grant; Tier-3 refusal on ETag mismatch (`STATE_DRIFT_ETAG` written); Tier-3 success path with `change_marker_at_propose == emails.change_marker`; lenient `mark_read` succeeds on a moved-but-existing email; `MOVE_TO_TRIAGE_FOLDER` succeeds on re-resolved target

**Given** the drainer is running in the worker
**When** the worker writes its heartbeat
**Then** `worker_health` is updated with `(component="drainer", last_heartbeat_at, last_outcome, last_error)` per Story 1.8's pattern
**And** drainer failures (entire loop crash) are caught and logged without exiting the worker

### Story 4.5: Outlook write-back via Graph + error-classified retry chain

As Adam,
I want `mailbot_api/sync/graph_client.py` extended with `apply_action_to_graph(action_row) → GraphApplyResult` covering every action_type with Graph effect, wrapped in the error-classified retry chain from AR-D5-1 (429/503/timeout → exponential backoff 1s/4s/16s max 3 retries; 4xx non-429 → immediate fail; 5xx non-503 → 1 retry then fail),
So that real Outlook write-back works for the full action roster with documented retry semantics, and the Tier-3 send-budget circuit breaker (AR-D5-2) flips `budget_consumed` correctly on both success and terminal failure.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 4.4's drainer dispatches via a `GraphWriteAdapter` interface
**When** `apply_action_to_graph(action_row: PendingActionRow) -> GraphApplyResult` is implemented
**Then** the function handles each action_type via a documented Graph endpoint mapping:

- `MARK_READ` / `MARK_UNREAD` → `PATCH /me/messages/{id}` with `{"isRead": true/false}`
- `ARCHIVE` → `POST /me/messages/{id}/move` with Archive folder id
- `MARK_JUNK` → `POST /me/messages/{id}/move` with JunkEmail folder id
- `MOVE_TO_USER_FOLDER` / `MOVE_TO_TRIAGE_FOLDER` / `MOVE_TO_INBOX` → `POST /me/messages/{id}/move` with target folder id (from `payload`)
- `ADD_LOCAL_CATEGORY` / `REMOVE_LOCAL_CATEGORY` → local SQL only (NOT synced to Outlook per FR-5.1) — returns success immediately without Graph call
- `DELETE` → `DELETE /me/messages/{id}` (soft-deletes to Deleted Items per Graph default)
- `SEND_REPLY` → `POST /me/messages/{id}/reply` with body from `payload`
- `SEND_NEW_EMAIL` → `POST /me/sendMail` with full message envelope from `payload`
- `SEND_FORWARD` → `POST /me/messages/{id}/forward` with body + recipients from `payload`
- `UNSUBSCRIBE` → executes the List-Unsubscribe header (HTTP POST to mailto: or http: URL from `payload`)
- `MODIFY_INBOX_RULE` / `MODIFY_OUTLOOK_FILTER` → Graph `POST/PATCH /me/mailFolders/inbox/messageRules` with payload
- `TOUCH_DELEGATED_MAILBOX` → Graph `/users/{upn}/*` endpoints with payload
- `REPLY_TO_INACTIVE_THREAD` → same as `SEND_REPLY` but with a precondition check that the thread's `last_received_at` is older than N days (N from payload, default 30)

**Given** any Graph call is in flight
**When** the response status is checked
**Then** **429 Too Many Requests** → respect `Retry-After` header (or default 1s if absent), retry up to 3 times with exponential backoff (1s → 4s → 16s)
**And** **503 Service Unavailable** → same retry policy as 429
**And** **timeout (>30s for write calls)** → retry up to 3 times with exponential backoff
**And** **5xx non-503** → 1 retry, then `GraphApplyResult(ok=False, error="provider_5xx", retry_count=1)`
**And** **4xx non-429** → immediate `GraphApplyResult(ok=False, error="provider_4xx_<status>", retry_count=0)`, no retry
**And** **network/transport error** → treated as transient, follows 429/503 retry policy

**Given** a Tier-3 SEND_REPLY successfully leaves
**When** the result is returned
**Then** `GraphApplyResult(ok=True, retry_count=<final n>)` is returned
**And** the drainer (Story 4.4) sets `budget_consumed=true` and increments the daily send count

**Given** a Tier-3 SEND_REPLY terminally fails after the retry chain exhausts
**When** the result is returned
**Then** `GraphApplyResult(ok=False, error=<sanitized message>, retry_count=3)` is returned
**And** the drainer sets `status="failed"`, `failure_reason=<error>`, `budget_consumed=true` (AR-D5-2 — failed sends consume budget too — circuit breaker prevents retry-bombing the cap)

**Given** a `status="failed"` row sits in `pending_actions`
**When** `mailbot replay <action_id>` (CLI extension to Story 1.8's placeholder shell) is invoked
**Then** if the row's `terminal_at` is within the last 7 days AND the original grant is still valid OR a fresh grant exists, the row is re-queued (`status="pending"`, `retry_count=0`, `terminal_at=NULL`, `failure_reason=NULL`)
**And** if the original grant has expired, the CLI refuses with a message pointing at `mint_grant`
**And** the replay event is logged with `event="action.replayed"`

**Given** `tests/integration/test_graph_write_retries.py` is implemented (gated by `OUTLOOK_TEST_ACCOUNT` env var — opt-in)
**When** the test exercises mark_read against a real Outlook test row
**Then** the row's `isRead` flips in Outlook (verifiable via a follow-up GET)
**And** `tests/unit/sync/test_graph_retries.py` (uses httpx mock) covers: 429 + Retry-After respected; 503 retried 3×; 4xx no retry; timeout → 3 retries; transport error → 3 retries

### Story 4.6: Cooling-off + `/cancel <action_id>` + hard 20-send/day cap enforcement

As Adam,
I want Tier-3 send rows to sit in `status="cooling_off"` for 60 seconds (configurable) before transitioning to `status="pending"` (drainer-eligible), `/cancel <action_id>` to mark the row `cancelled` if still in cooling_off, and the drainer to enforce the hard 20-send/day cap by refusing to drain any send-family row once the day's `budget_consumed=true` count for send-family actions hits 20,
So that "wait, not that one" moments are recoverable and a hijacked agent cannot spam Adam's contacts even with a valid grant.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `mailbot_api/actions/cooling_off.py` is implemented and joins the worker process
**When** the cooling-off ticker runs (every 1s)
**Then** it queries `SELECT id FROM pending_actions WHERE status = 'cooling_off' AND proposed_at <= now() - INTERVAL '60 seconds'`
**And** for each matching row, atomically transitions `status="cooling_off" → "pending"` with `UPDATE pending_actions SET status='pending' WHERE id = ? AND status='cooling_off'` (the `AND status='cooling_off'` guard prevents a race with `/cancel`)
**And** the cooling-off duration is read from `policy.yaml` (new field `pending_send_cooling_off_seconds`, default 60) so Adam can tune it per the FR-5.3 "configurable to 0" requirement

**Given** `mailbot_api/verbs/cancel_action.py` is implemented
**When** `cancel_action(action_id: int) -> CancelActionOut` is called via MCP
**Then** the verb atomically transitions `UPDATE pending_actions SET status='cancelled', terminal_at=now() WHERE id = ? AND status='cooling_off'`
**And** the affected-row count determines the response: 1 row → `CancelActionOut(ok=True, cancelled=True)`; 0 rows → `CancelActionOut(ok=True, cancelled=False, reason="action no longer in cooling_off")` (covers cases where the action already drained, failed, or was previously cancelled)
**And** the structured log line `event="action.cancelled"` is emitted with `action_id`

**Given** the worker scheduler ticks
**When** the cooling-off ticker AND `/cancel` arrive in the same second
**Then** SQLite's row-level atomicity guarantees exactly one wins (per the `AND status='cooling_off'` guards) — verifiable by a stress test in `tests/integration/test_cancel_race.py` that fires 100 simultaneous cancel + cooling-off transitions

**Given** the drainer from Story 4.4 picks up a `SEND_*` row
**When** the per-row checks include the daily send cap
**Then** the drainer queries `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM pending_actions WHERE action_type IN ('send_reply', 'send_new_email', 'send_forward', 'reply_to_inactive_thread') AND budget_consumed = 1 AND terminal_at >= <today UTC midnight>`
**And** if the count is `>= 20`, the drainer marks this row `status="failed"`, `failure_reason="daily_send_cap_exceeded"`, `budget_consumed=true` (consumes one slot for the failed attempt — prevents retry-bombing) and emits an urgent chat alert (FR-5.4 hard cap)
**And** if the count is `< 20`, the drainer proceeds normally

**Given** the cap enforcement is in place
**When** `tests/unit/actions/test_send_cap.py` runs
**Then** scenarios are covered: 19 successful sends today + 1 new send → 20th send proceeds; 20 sends today + 1 new send → 21st refused immediately; 19 successful + 1 failed (counts as consumed) + 1 new send → 21st refused; midnight UTC rollover resets the counter

**Given** the `policy.yaml` has `pending_send_cooling_off_seconds: 0`
**When** a Tier-3 send is proposed
**Then** it's inserted with `status="cooling_off"` but immediately eligible for drain (the 1s ticker promotes it on the next tick)
**And** `/cancel` essentially has no window — documented behavior, not a bug

**Given** the cooling-off + cancel mechanism is in place
**When** `tests/integration/test_cooling_off_cancel.py` exercises the full flow
**Then** a SEND_REPLY proposed at T=0 reaches `status="pending"` no earlier than T=60s
**And** `cancel_action(id)` called at T=30s yields `cancelled=True`; called at T=70s yields `cancelled=False` (already promoted to pending)
**And** the `notifications_pending` file gets the urgent alert on cap breach

### Story 4.7: Sensitive-content API override handshake — `mint_sensitivity_token` + `confirmation_token` parameter + in-memory registry

As Adam,
I want `mint_sensitivity_token(email_id, task_type) → token` as a Tier-0 verb (refuses if `emails.sensitivity == 'confidential'`), an in-memory token registry with 10-minute TTL in `mailbot_api/actions/sensitivity_tokens.py`, the `ask_router(..., confirmation_token=None)` parameter, and the Router precondition layer that consumes the token on a single sensitive-to-API call and writes `sensitivity_grant_id` + `sensitivity_grant_minted_at` to `router_calls`,
So that every sensitive-to-API call carries a one-to-one mint/consume audit pair and `confidential` emails admit no override path at all.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `mailbot_api/verbs/mint_sensitivity_token.py` is implemented
**When** `mint_sensitivity_token(email_id: str, task_type: str) -> MintSensitivityTokenOut` is called via MCP
**Then** the verb reads `emails.sensitivity` for `email_id`
**And** if `emails.sensitivity == 'confidential'`, refuses with `MintSensitivityTokenOut(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=SENSITIVITY_BLOCKS_API, message="confidential emails admit no API override"))` — no mint happens, no audit row written
**And** if `emails.sensitivity == 'normal'`, refuses with `MintSensitivityTokenOut(ok=False, error=...message="email is not sensitive; no token required")` — normal emails don't need tokens
**And** if `emails.sensitivity == 'sensitive'`, mints a fresh token: generates a cryptographically random 32-byte token (`secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`), inserts into the in-memory registry keyed by `(token, email_id, task_type, expires_at=now()+10min, consumed=False)`
**And** emits a structured log line `event="sensitivity.token.minted"` with `email_id`, `task_type`, `grant_id=<the token's short hash for audit linkage>`, `expires_at` — no token material is logged
**And** returns `MintSensitivityTokenOut(ok=True, token=<value>, expires_at=<value>, grant_id=<hash>)`

**Given** `mailbot_api/actions/sensitivity_tokens.py` is implemented
**When** the in-memory registry is constructed
**Then** it's a process-local `dict[str, SensitivityToken]` (per AR-D12-1 — dies on worker process restart by design, forcing re-confirmation)
**And** the registry runs a periodic sweep (every 60s) removing expired tokens (`expires_at < now()`) — keeps the dict bounded
**And** `consume_token(token, email_id, task_type) -> str | None` returns the token's `grant_id` hash if a matching row exists, is unexpired, and `consumed=False` — then marks it `consumed=True`; returns `None` otherwise
**And** the registry is **not** persisted to SQLite — only the audit trail on `router_calls` survives (AR-D12-2)

**Given** Story 2.4's `ask_router(...)` signature
**When** the signature is extended in this story
**Then** `ask_router(task_type, content, force_model=None, confirmation_token=None, max_cost_usd=None, email_id=None, caller_origin="verb-ask-router", caller_verb=None, force=False) -> RouterResult` is the new public surface
**And** the parameter is forwarded to the Router precondition layer

**Given** Story 3.3's Router precondition layer
**When** an `ask_router` call attempts to dispatch a Tier-2/3-equivalent API call (Haiku or Opus) on an `email_id` whose `emails.sensitivity == 'sensitive'`
**Then** the precondition layer checks `confirmation_token`: if `None`, refuse with `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=NEEDS_SENSITIVITY_CONFIRMATION, message="sensitive email requires per-session confirmation token to escalate to API"))`
**And** if a token is provided, call `consume_token(token, email_id, task_type)`:

- returns `None` (invalid/expired/consumed) → refuse with `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=NEEDS_SENSITIVITY_CONFIRMATION, message="confirmation token invalid, expired, or already consumed"))`
- returns `grant_id` → allow dispatch, and on the resulting `record_router_call`, populate `sensitivity_grant_id=<grant_id>` and `sensitivity_grant_minted_at=<token's mint timestamp>` columns

**Given** an `ask_router` call targets a `confidential` email
**When** the precondition layer runs
**Then** any API-bound call refuses with `RouterResult(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=SENSITIVITY_BLOCKS_API, ...))` regardless of any `confirmation_token` (confidential admits no override per NFR-PRIV-2)

**Given** the full handshake is in place
**When** `tests/unit/actions/test_sensitivity_tokens.py` runs
**Then** scenarios are covered: mint on `normal` refused; mint on `confidential` refused; mint on `sensitive` succeeds; consume with correct (token, email_id, task_type) succeeds and returns grant_id; consume with mismatched task_type fails; consume after 10-min TTL fails; second consume of same token fails (single-use); process restart wipes registry (verified by unit test simulating registry reconstruction)

**Given** the handshake is in place
**When** `tests/integration/test_router_sensitivity_audit.py` runs end-to-end
**Then** a mint → ask_router(confirmation_token=...) → record_router_call cycle produces a `router_calls` row with `sensitivity_grant_id` and `sensitivity_grant_minted_at` populated
**And** the `event="sensitivity.token.minted"` log line and the `event="router.call.ok"` log line both carry the `grant_id` for cross-referencing

### Story 4.8: Tier-1 24-hour reverter via `action_history`

As Adam,
I want every successful Tier-1 action's pre-state captured in `action_history`, plus a `revert_action(action_id) → RevertOut` verb and `mailbot revert <action_id>` CLI that reverses the action by re-applying the pre-state if within 24h,
So that "you marked the wrong thread as read" is a single command, and the Tier-1 reversibility promise (AR-D6-3 `reversibility_window_hours=24`) is real, not aspirational.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 4.4's drainer writes `action_history` rows before Graph dispatch
**When** the drainer applies a Tier-1 action
**Then** the `action_history.pre_state` JSON captures the field(s) the action will modify (e.g., `{"isRead": false}` for a `MARK_READ`; `{"folder_id": "<previous folder>"}` for `MOVE_TO_TRIAGE_FOLDER`; `{"categories": [<previous list>]}` for `ADD_LOCAL_CATEGORY`)
**And** the snapshot is written via `db.execute_write` in the same transaction as `status="applied"` (atomic — no orphan history rows)

**Given** `mailbot_api/actions/reverter.py` is implemented
**When** `revert_action(action_id: int) -> RevertOut` is called via MCP
**Then** the verb reads the `pending_actions` row and the corresponding `action_history` row
**And** if `pending_actions.tier != 1`, refuses with `RevertOut(ok=False, error="only Tier-1 actions are auto-revertible; higher tiers require a fresh action proposal")`
**And** if `pending_actions.status != 'applied'`, refuses with `RevertOut(ok=False, error="only applied actions can be reverted (current status: <X>)")`
**And** if `pending_actions.terminal_at + 24h < now()`, refuses with `RevertOut(ok=False, error="revert window of 24h has closed")`
**And** if `action_history.reverted_at IS NOT NULL`, refuses with `RevertOut(ok=False, error="action already reverted at <timestamp>")`
**And** otherwise: constructs a new action that re-applies `pre_state` (e.g., `MARK_UNREAD` if original was `MARK_READ`; `MOVE_TO_<previous_folder>` if original was `MOVE_TO_TRIAGE_FOLDER`), inserts it into `pending_actions` with `status="pending"` (Tier-1 — no grant needed), and on its successful drain marks `action_history.reverted_at = <now()>`
**And** emits `event="action.reverted"` log line with both `original_action_id` and `revert_action_id`

**Given** Story 1.8's `mailbot` CLI placeholder
**When** the CLI is extended with `mailbot revert <action_id>` in this story
**Then** the CLI invokes the verb via the local MCP server and prints the result
**And** non-zero exit on `ok=False`; zero exit on success

**Given** the reverter is in place
**When** `tests/integration/test_revert.py` exercises end-to-end revert scenarios against a mocked Graph
**Then** a `MARK_READ` action reverted within 1h succeeds and the email's `isRead` returns to false
**And** a `MOVE_TO_TRIAGE_FOLDER` action reverted re-resolves to the original folder
**And** an attempted revert on a Tier-2 action refuses
**And** an attempted revert at h=25 (mocked clock) refuses
**And** a double-revert refuses on the second attempt

**Given** the Epic-4 stack is complete
**When** Adam manually runs a smoke test against a real Outlook test account
**Then** propose → grant → cool-off → drain → apply → revert all work end-to-end
**And** a SEND_REPLY in cooling_off is cancellable
**And** a sensitive-classified email's draft_reply via Opus requires a `mint_sensitivity_token` first
**And** a `confidential` email's draft_reply via Opus is refused even with attempted token injection

---

## Epic 5 Detail — Conversational Control: Discord Chat as the Defender's Voice

This epic ships the Hermes integration end-to-end: Discord adapter, MCP server, the agent-facing verb library, the defender persona, the chat-side prompts, the chat-input redactor, and the draft-reply flow that ties Epic 5's chat surface to Epic 4's send mechanics. After this epic, Adam can DM the bot, get an answer within p95 ≤ 5s, drive a multi-turn refinement, and have the bot speak consistently across DM, channel, digest, and slash-command replies. FR-4.3's ≥ 90% reference-resolution threshold is *built and instrumented* here; *validation* against the dedicated 20-item eval slice lands in Epic 7.

### Story 5.1: Read-side verbs — projection-first data window for the agent

As Adam,
I want six read-side verbs (`find_emails`, `hydrate_email`, `get_thread`, `list_unread`, `count_emails`, `get_sender_summary`) implemented as plain Python functions returning `<Verb>Out` Pydantic models with projection-only data,
So that the agent's data window is structurally narrow (Rule J — hydration discipline), rate-limited per turn, and unit-testable in isolation before any MCP harness exists.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Stories 3.x have populated derived-field columns on `emails`
**When** `mailbot_api/verbs/find_emails.py` is implemented
**Then** `find_emails(filter: FindEmailsFilter, limit: int = 25) -> FindEmailsOut` returns a list of projections containing only: `email_id`, `received_at`, `from_address`, `from_display_name`, `subject`, `summary_short`, `class_coarse`, `importance_score`, `sensitivity`, `has_attachments`
**And** `FindEmailsFilter` is a Pydantic model accepting: `unread_only: bool = False`, `sender_address: str | None = None`, `sender_domain: str | None = None`, `class_coarse: str | None = None`, `importance_min: int | None = None`, `since: str | None = None` (UTC ISO-8601), `until: str | None = None`, `query: str | None = None` (full-text on subject + summary_short)
**And** the verb refuses `limit > 100` with a `FindEmailsOut(ok=False, error=...)` carrying the message "limit capped at 100 — use repeated queries with the `since` filter if you need more"
**And** every returned projection is the agent-visible row; full bodies require a separate `hydrate_email` call (Rule J)

**Given** `mailbot_api/verbs/hydrate_email.py` is implemented
**When** `hydrate_email(email_id: str) -> HydrateEmailOut` is called
**Then** the verb returns the full body (raw + derived) for one email: subject, body_text, body_html (sanitized — no remote images), all derived fields with companion metadata
**And** the verb is rate-limited to 5 calls per agent turn (Rule J — hydration discipline). The 5/turn limit is enforced server-side via a per-session counter (Hermes session ID passed in MCP context); exceeding it returns `HydrateEmailOut(ok=False, error=RouterError(code=RATE_LIMITED, message="hydration limit is 5 emails per turn — narrow your filter first"))`
**And** the verb refuses on `confidential`-classified emails when the MCP caller is the agent (the body never reaches the agent's prompt context); returns `HydrateEmailOut(ok=False, error=...message="confidential emails cannot be hydrated to the agent — only metadata is available")`

**Given** `mailbot_api/verbs/get_thread.py` is implemented
**When** `get_thread(thread_id: str) -> GetThreadOut` is called
**Then** the verb returns the thread's ordered list of email projections (same shape as `find_emails`), the cached `thread_continuity_note` from Story 3.7, and the thread's message count
**And** the verb does not hydrate bodies (caller follows up with `hydrate_email` per email_id if needed)

**Given** `mailbot_api/verbs/list_unread.py`, `mailbot_api/verbs/count_emails.py`, and `mailbot_api/verbs/get_sender_summary.py` are implemented
**When** each is called
**Then** `list_unread(limit=25)` returns projections of unread emails ordered by `received_at DESC`
**And** `count_emails(filter: FindEmailsFilter)` returns `CountEmailsOut(count: int)` — no projections, just the count (cheap signal for "how many?")
**And** `get_sender_summary(sender_address: str)` returns `GetSenderSummaryOut(sender_address, display_name, message_count, last_seen_at, sender_reputation_summary)` from the `senders` table

**Given** all six verbs are implemented
**When** `tests/unit/verbs/test_read_verbs.py` parametrizes over the verb set
**Then** every verb's input model rejects malformed inputs with `<Verb>Out(ok=False, error=...)` (never raises)
**And** every verb's `<Verb>Out` model has `ok: bool`, `error: RouterError | None`, and the success payload fields documented above
**And** Rule J enforcement is tested: 6th `hydrate_email` in a session returns `RATE_LIMITED`; `confidential`-hydration refused; oversized `limit` refused

### Story 5.2: MCP server exposing verbs as tools

As Adam,
I want `mailbot_api/mcp_server.py` to expose every verb in `mailbot_api/verbs/` as an MCP tool using FastMCP from the MCP Python SDK 1.27.2, with the `<Verb>In` schema becoming the tool input schema and verb descriptions populated from the verb's docstring,
So that Hermes can connect as an MCP client, discover the tools, and invoke them in agent turns with the same data shapes used in Python tests.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 5.1's verbs are implemented and Story 1.1 pinned `mcp==1.27.2`
**When** `mailbot_api/mcp_server.py` is implemented using `FastMCP`
**Then** every verb in `mailbot_api/verbs/` is registered as an MCP tool via the `@mcp.tool()` decorator
**And** each tool's input schema is the verb's `<Verb>In` Pydantic model (auto-derived JSON schema)
**And** each tool's description is a one-sentence summary lifted from the verb function's docstring — explicitly including cost-relevant constraints ("Rate-limited to 5 calls per chat turn" on `hydrate_email`, "Capped at 100 results" on `find_emails`, "Rule J — projections only" on each read verb)
**And** the MCP server runs as part of the `uvicorn` process (per Story 1.2) on the same port (8000) under a documented MCP path prefix

**Given** the MCP server is running
**When** an MCP client connects (test harness using the MCP SDK's client)
**Then** the client can list all registered tools and see the documented input schemas
**And** the client can invoke any tool with valid input and receive the verb's `<Verb>Out` model serialized over MCP
**And** the client receives errors as MCP error responses for protocol-level failures; verb-level errors (the `error` field on `<Verb>Out`) are returned as data (AR-PAT-4 error-as-data — never raise across the MCP boundary)

**Given** the server is up
**When** the agent (via Hermes) calls a tool and the verb returns `<Verb>Out(ok=False, error=...)`
**Then** the agent sees the structured `error` field in the tool response
**And** `AGENTS.md` (Story 5.5) instructs the agent to inspect `result.error` after every tool call

**Given** the server is in place
**When** `tests/integration/test_mcp_server.py` exercises a full client-server round-trip for each verb
**Then** every verb is discoverable, invocable, and returns the expected shape
**And** schema mismatches in input are surfaced as MCP-level validation errors (not silent dispatches)

### Story 5.3: Chat-side prompts — `intent_parsing_chat`, `reference_resolution`, `draft_reply`, `tone_style_mirror`, `multi_turn_refinement`

As Adam,
I want five chat-side prompt modules written per the AR-PAT-5 structure, with policy.yaml assignments per Rule G (interactive default = API; draft_reply force-routes to Opus per FR-4.4),
So that the chat surface has the prompts it needs before Hermes starts routing Discord traffic.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 3.2's AR-PAT-5 prompt structure is established
**When** each of `prompts/intent_parsing_chat/v1.py`, `prompts/reference_resolution/v1.py`, `prompts/draft_reply/v1.py`, `prompts/tone_style_mirror/v1.py`, `prompts/multi_turn_refinement/v1.py` is created
**Then** each module exports `VERSION`, `SYSTEM`, `USER_TEMPLATE`, `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` per AR-PAT-5

**Given** `prompts/intent_parsing_chat/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `intent: Literal["find_emails", "list_unread", "summarize_thread", "draft_reply", "count_query", "send_action", "delete_action", "mute_category", "label_emails", "small_talk", "ambiguous"]`, `target_email_ids: list[str]` (may be empty when intent doesn't target specific emails), `confidence: float`, `proposed_filter: FindEmailsFilter | None` (when intent maps to a find/count query, the parsed filter)

**Given** `prompts/reference_resolution/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `resolved_email_ids: list[str]`, `reasoning: str` (≤ 200 chars — what made the agent pick these IDs), `confidence: float`, `ambiguous: bool` (True when the resolution had to guess between multiple plausible candidates)

**Given** `prompts/draft_reply/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `draft_body: str` (the reply text in Adam's tone — informed by `tone_style_mirror` output if available), `suggested_subject: str`, `tone_signals_used: list[str]` (≤ 5 — what stylistic cues the model picked up from prior sent emails), `defender_warnings: list[str]` (anything the defender persona wants Adam to double-check before sending — e.g., "this reply commits to a deadline; confirm you can meet it")
**And** `policy.yaml[draft_reply]` is annotated with `model: claude-opus-4-7`, `escalate: false`, `max_tokens_out: 800`, `lane: interactive`, `sensitivity: any` (sensitivity-token handshake from Story 4.7 gates `sensitive` emails), `notes: "Opus per FR-4.4 - draft quality is a tier-1 product capability"`, `demotion_hypothesis: "DEMOTE to Haiku once F8 measures Adam's edit-distance is similar"`

**Given** `prompts/tone_style_mirror/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `tone_attributes: list[str]` (e.g., ["concise", "formal", "uses_first_names", "avoids_emoji"]), `signature_pattern: str | None` (Adam's typical sign-off pattern if detectable), `salutation_pattern: str | None`
**And** the prompt routes to Opus per policy.yaml — but is invoked ONCE per recipient and cached against `(recipient_address, prompt_version, model)` via the response cache from Story 2.7 (`response_cache_ttl_seconds: 2592000` — 30 days; tone evolves slowly)

**Given** `prompts/multi_turn_refinement/v1.py`
**When** its `OUTPUT_SCHEMA` is inspected
**Then** the schema requires `refined_draft: str`, `changes_summary: str` (≤ 200 chars), `still_needs_clarification: bool` (True when the refinement instruction was itself ambiguous and the model wants Adam to clarify)
**And** the prompt routes to Opus per policy.yaml

**Given** the prompt modules are in place
**When** `tests/unit/prompts/test_chat_prompts.py` parametrizes over the five modules
**Then** every module loads cleanly through the Story 3.2 registry; every OUTPUT_SCHEMA round-trips against a sample; every USER_TEMPLATE accepts its documented placeholders

### Story 5.4: Hermes container config + Discord adapter + MCP client wiring

As Adam,
I want `hermes-config/config.yaml` configured so Hermes uses `http://mailbot-api:8000/v1` as its custom LLM provider (covering `provider`, `auxiliary.compression`, and `auxiliary.title_generation`), connects to `mailbot-api`'s MCP server as a client, and exposes the Discord adapter against my bot token,
So that I can DM the bot "hello" and the message routes through Hermes → mailbot-api Router → Qwen, with the response delivered to Discord in the defender voice.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 1.2's Hermes container runs `nousresearch/hermes-agent:latest` with `/opt/data` mounted from the `mailbot_hermes_data` named volume
**When** `hermes-config/config.yaml` is written
**Then** the `provider` block points `base_url: http://mailbot-api:8000/v1`, `model: <default model id e.g. "qwen-router-default">` (the model name is a Router-internal alias — mapping lives in policy.yaml), `api_key: ${MAILBOT_ROUTER_KEY}` (read from env)
**And** the `auxiliary.compression.provider: custom` and `auxiliary.title_generation.provider: custom` both point at the same base_url with header `X-Mailbot-Caller-Origin: hermes-aux-compression` and `hermes-aux-title` respectively (per Story 2.10's `caller_origin` design)
**And** the `fallback_providers` block carries one entry pointing directly at `api.anthropic.com` with `claude-opus-4-7` as the safety net (NFR-OPS-6 — emergency only, bypasses Router cost discipline by design)
**And** the `gateway.discord` block carries `bot_token: ${DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN}` and the bot's intent flags configured for DM + slash commands
**And** the `mcp_clients` block carries one entry connecting to `http://mailbot-api:8000/mcp` (per Story 5.2)

**Given** Hermes is configured
**When** the `mailbot-hermes` container starts
**Then** it successfully authenticates the Discord bot
**And** the bot appears in Adam's private Discord server with a "MailBot" presence
**And** Hermes's MCP client connects to mailbot-api and discovers the tool list from Story 5.2

**Given** Hermes is running
**When** Adam DMs the bot "hello"
**Then** the message routes through Hermes → `POST /v1/chat/completions` on `mailbot-api` with `X-Mailbot-Caller-Origin: hermes-aux-compression` (or whatever Hermes uses for its main inference path)
**And** the Router dispatches to Qwen per `policy.yaml` (the default chat path)
**And** Hermes posts the response to Discord within p95 ≤ 5s
**And** a `router_calls` row records the dispatch with `caller_origin` populated

**Given** the container topology is up
**When** `tests/integration/test_hermes_routing.py` runs (gated by env vars for live Discord + Anthropic)
**Then** a DM round-trip completes successfully
**And** the corresponding `router_calls` row's `caller_origin` matches the Hermes-configured value

### Story 5.5: `SOUL.md` defender persona + `AGENTS.md` operational rules + `SKILL.md`

As Adam,
I want `hermes-config/SOUL.md` to encode the defender identity (conservative, quiet, asks before destructive actions, shows reasoning when proposing actions), `hermes-config/AGENTS.md` to translate Rules J / N / P / R into agent behavior (hydration discipline, cost-aware verb choice, tier-aware proposals, notification tiering), and `hermes-config/skills/mailbot/SKILL.md` to document how the agent should use the verb surface,
So that the bot's voice reads consistently across DM, channel, digest, alerts, and slash-command replies — and the agent's behavior follows the same rules everywhere.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** `hermes-config/SOUL.md` is written
**When** the persona doc is loaded by Hermes at startup
**Then** it explicitly establishes: identity ("I am MailBot, a defender of your attention — not an inbox assistant"); voice (conservative, terse, defender-toned, avoids cheerfulness when not appropriate, never apologetic-when-unnecessary); destructive-action posture (asks before deleting, sends, or moving anything beyond Triage; surfaces reasoning when proposing); quiet bias (no unsolicited "just checking in" messages; no idle chit-chat)
**And** the doc explicitly names the four behavioral anti-patterns from NFR-PERSONA-2 as banned: never send without per-message authorization; never delete without per-action authorization; never quote sensitive content outside chat; never produce noisy notifications

**Given** `hermes-config/AGENTS.md` is written
**When** the operational rules doc is loaded
**Then** it documents — as agent-facing instructions, not just human prose — how Rules J / N / P / R manifest:

- **Rule J (Hydration Discipline):** "Use `find_emails` and `list_unread` first; only call `hydrate_email` when you need the full body. You are rate-limited to 5 hydrations per turn. If you need more, refine your filter."
- **Rule N (Cost Discipline):** "Default to the cheapest tier. Cite a reason in your own reasoning trace when you escalate to Opus via `force_model`. Never call `force_model` without explicit user request or a documented benchmark justification."
- **Rule P (Authorization Tiers):** "Tier-1 actions (`MARK_READ`, `ADD_LOCAL_CATEGORY`, etc.) execute automatically. Tier-2 (`ARCHIVE`, `MARK_JUNK`) require a batch grant via `mint_grant`. Tier-3 (`DELETE`, `SEND_*`) require explicit per-action confirmation. Always propose with `propose_action`; never claim a tier — the verb decides."
- **Rule R (Notification Tiering):** "When sending the user a chat message, classify it: urgent (push immediately), important (batch for the 8am digest), informational (only on request), silent (log only). Default to silent unless the user is being asked something."

**Given** `hermes-config/skills/mailbot/SKILL.md` is written
**When** the skill doc is loaded
**Then** it walks through the verb surface (read verbs → action verbs → router verbs) with example turn structures: "user asks 'show me unread' → call `list_unread` → format projection → respond"; "user asks 'draft a reply to that' → call `reference_resolution` → call `ask_router(task_type='draft_reply', ...)` → present draft → wait for confirmation → call `propose_action(..., action_type=ActionType.SEND_REPLY)`"
**And** the skill doc explicitly warns: "Never call `ask_router(task_type='draft_reply', ...)` on a `sensitive`-classified email without first calling `mint_sensitivity_token` and passing the result as `confirmation_token`"

**Given** the persona + rules + skill are in place
**When** four sample interactions are run end-to-end through Hermes (DM "hello", DM "what's important today", channel `/cost`, urgent notification flow stub-tested by triggering Story 1.8's sync-stale path)
**Then** all four responses read in a consistent defender voice (no cheerful "assistant" tone), no banned anti-pattern is triggered (manual review of the four outputs)

### Story 5.6: Slash command dispatcher

As Adam,
I want Discord slash commands `/cost`, `/pause`, `/resume`, `/cancel <action_id>`, `/mute <category>`, `/label <recent>`, `/budget reset`, and `/confirm <email_id> <task_type>` registered with the Discord bot and dispatched to their corresponding MCP verbs, with each command working identically in DM and shared-server channel,
So that the slash-command UIs promised in Stories 2.8, 2.9, 2.10, 4.6, and 4.7 finally have their human-facing surface.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Story 5.4's Hermes Discord adapter is running
**When** `hermes-config/config.yaml`'s Discord adapter section is extended with the slash command list
**Then** Hermes registers the following slash commands with Discord on startup: `/cost [today|month]`, `/pause [reason]`, `/resume`, `/cancel <action_id>`, `/mute <category>`, `/label <recent> <label>`, `/budget reset`, `/confirm <email_id> <task_type>`
**And** each command's argument schema is documented in Discord's slash command UI (autocomplete + descriptions)

**Given** the slash commands are registered
**When** any of them is invoked in DM
**Then** the command dispatches to its corresponding verb via MCP: `/cost` → `cost_breakdown(period)` from Story 2.10; `/pause` → `pause_router(reason)` from Story 2.9; `/resume` → `resume_router()` from Story 2.9; `/cancel` → `cancel_action(action_id)` from Story 4.6; `/mute` → `set_mute(category, until)` (verb stub here; Epic 6 implements the routing-by-category logic); `/label` → `add_local_category(email_ids, label)` (calls `propose_action` with `ActionType.ADD_LOCAL_CATEGORY` per email_id from the recent context); `/budget reset` → `budget_reset()` from Story 2.8; `/confirm` → `mint_sensitivity_token(email_id, task_type)` from Story 4.7

**Given** the same slash command is invoked in a shared server channel
**When** the dispatcher runs
**Then** the command behaves identically to the DM invocation (FR-4.8) — same verb, same response shape
**And** the response is posted as an ephemeral reply (visible only to Adam) for commands that surface cost, budget, or sensitivity information (`/cost`, `/budget reset`, `/confirm`), preventing accidental disclosure if the server has other members

**Given** `/confirm` is invoked for a `confidential` email
**When** the dispatcher calls `mint_sensitivity_token`
**Then** the verb refuses per Story 4.7 (`SENSITIVITY_BLOCKS_API`)
**And** the slash command response posts a defender-toned message: "Confidential emails admit no API override. The body stays on your VPS, period."

**Given** `/mute` is invoked
